### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment agent" triggers an auto-DM with "all my Claude Code guides + skills", very likely links to his **free Skool community** (templates/prompts) and from there an upsell to **Chase AI+ ($99/mo)**, his YouTube, and his own repo claudex-loop. Chase Hannegan also sells AI-agent/n8n consulting and 1:1 mentorship (chaseai.io). All 10 repos are public; the GitHub paths are listed above, so there is no need to comment.

### Verification & insights
- All 10 repos verified with git ls-remote and shallow clones in scratchpad/repos (no code executed). All are active (last commits Oct 2026).
- **OmniRoute risk:** chaining free tiers to get "billions of tokens for free" depends on each provider's terms. Routing consumer-subscription OAuth logins (Claude Code, Antigravity, etc.) through a third-party gateway can breach those services' ToS and risk account bans, and you hand a local proxy your credentials. The README itself stresses the tokens are not provided by OmniRoute.
- AGPL (OpenMontage) has network-use copyleft obligations if you deploy a modified version as a service.
- DeepSeek Harness is a "developer preview" with a safety notice; treat it as unstable.
- Several repos (Omarchy, ECC-style harnesses) overlap with other posts; deepseek-harness, omarchy, archify and OpenMontage were already cloned for earlier posts.
- Could not verify: "fastest growing repo of all time", anydoc being "more accurate than anyone else" (vendor benchmark only), and the post date.

## DdXnIxhl32v | @agentic.james | "MAC OS IS DEAD": a hype reel about Omarchy 4 ("Quattro"), DHH's open-source, agent-friendly Arch/Hyprland Linux distro with plugins, an Omarchy agent skill and a CLI
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DdXnIxhl32v/
- **Account:** @agentic.james (James Goldbach) · **Posted:** 2026-09-17 · **Type:** reel (41.7s, 22 scene pages)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram, fetched with yt-dlp)
- **Category:** GitHub & dev
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE. Omarchy is real, MIT-licensed and actively developed (basecamp/omarchy, last commit 2026-10-20). The "agent-native" features shown (plugins, a bundled agent skill, agent launchers and usage tracking) exist in the repo. "macOS is dead" is clickbait: Omarchy is a full Linux install that replaces your OS on a PC, not a macOS replacement for Apple Silicon Macs. The creator himself says he will "prolly still use Mac too".

### Post description
> Pretty sick, prolly still gonna use Mac too

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
There is no call to action, link or product. It is an engagement post ("should I make a long-form video?"). Omarchy itself is free.

### Verification & insights
- I shallow-cloned basecamp/omarchy into scratchpad/repos/basecamp__omarchy and read the README, LICENSE (MIT) and manual/17-ai.md. The bin/ directory shows the plugin and agent commands; default/agents/skills holds the agent skills. Nothing was executed.
- Claim check: "built on Linux, opinionated Linux" is true (Arch plus Hyprland). Plugins, CLI, skills and agent session and usage tracking are all true. "Killed macOS" is hype: it is a Linux distro that you install from an ISO, wiping a drive (encrypted by default). It is aimed at PC hardware; Apple Silicon Macs are not a mainstream target.
- The manual warns that agents launched through `omarchy agent prompt` run "unattended in their respective don't-stop-to-ask modes". Giving an agent root-adjacent control of your OS config carries real risk.
- I could not verify the live star count, the "v4.0.4" release tag or the "Radio Atlas" plugin author.

## DejYP0FEZp3 | @vassity.ai | Infisical: open-source secrets-management platform (README scroll + voiceover), "comment INFISICAL"
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DejYP0FEZp3/
- **Account:** @vassity.ai (Vassity | AI) · **Posted:** 2026-10-16 · **Type:** reel (48.6s, 1 scene page: a slow scroll through the GitHub README)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram; fetched via yt-dlp on the second attempt after a 429)
- **Category:** GitHub & dev
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE (core) / LEGIT – PAID (some features). Infisical is a real, widely used secrets manager (github.com/Infisical/infisical). The core is MIT and self-hostable. Many features shown on screen (dynamic secrets, secret rotation, honey tokens, Agent Vault, PAM, point-in-time recovery, audit logs, secret scanning) live in the `ee/` directory, which needs a paid Infisical enterprise license.

### Post description

>
> Sync environment variables across your team, CI/CD pipelines, and cloud infrastructure seamlessly. 🤯

>

### Audio
A voiceover, possibly synthetic. Whisper wrote "InPhysical".
- [0.0–18.8] "Infisical is a free open source secret management platform that synchronizes environment variables and credentials securely across your entire engineering team. Sharing sensitive API keys over chat apps or storing unencrypted .env files and local folders leads to catastrophic credential leaks."
- [18.8–29.3] "Infisical provides an end-to-end encrypted dashboard with role-based access control, secret versioning and automatic synchronization to GitHub Actions, Docker and Kubernetes."
- [29.3–41.0] "Users can inject secrets directly into their local processes using a lightweight CLI without ever saving plain text keys to disk. Self-host using Docker Compose to ensure your cryptographic keys never leave your infrastructure."
- [41.0–48.4] "Perfect for growing software teams prioritizing zero-trust developer security. Comment Infisical and I will send you the repo."

### On-screen content, page by page
Fixed frame: header "**Infisical** / Developer Tool", a browser window at github.com, a "Vassity | AI" badge with the creator's photo, and a bottom bar "💡 Comment "INFISICAL" > Direct GitHub Link". The README scrolls from top to bottom over 48s (third-party README, summarized):

"Introduction": "Infisical is the open source security infrastructure platform that teams use for secrets, certificates, and privileged access management." "Features → **Secrets Management**: Dashboard; Secret Syncs (GitHub, Vercel, AWS, Terraform, Ansible…); Secret versioning and Point-in-Time Recovery; Secret Rotation (PostgreSQL, MySQL, AWS IAM…); Dynamic Secrets (PostgreSQL, MySQL, RabbitMQ…); Secret Scanning and Leak Prevention; Infisical Kubernetes Operator; Infisical Agent; Honey Tokens; Agent Vault."

- **≈40–48s:** PAM continued (Session Recording, Credential Rotation, Web Access to SSH/PostgreSQL/Redis/Windows RDP); **General Platform** (Authentication Methods: Kubernetes, GCP, Azure, AWS, OIDC, Universal Auth; Access Controls: RBAC, temporary access, approval workflows; Audit logs; Gateway; Self-hosting; SDKs: Node, Python, Go, Ruby, Java, .NET; CLI; API); "Getting started": Use Infisical Cloud / Deploy Infisical on premise; "Run Infisical locally".

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment INFISICAL" leads to a "Direct GitHub Link" DM, a free repo link used to build engagement. No pricing, affiliate or course is shown. The direct link is github.com/Infisical/infisical (cloud: infisical.com).

### Verification & insights
- Blobless clone: read the LICENSE and README and listed the `ee/` services. No code was run.
- "Free open source": true for the core (dashboard, secrets, syncs, CLI, injection, self-hosting). Not true for many headline features in the README scroll, which are enterprise or paid.
- "End-to-end encrypted": Infisical encrypts secrets, but the self-hosted server holds the keys (KMS / root encryption key). Treat "keys never leave your infrastructure" as true only when you self-host and manage the root key.
- "Inject secrets without saving plain text to disk" (`infisical run -- <cmd>`) is accurate.
- A legitimate, well-known project. The post adds nothing beyond the README.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment REPO" triggers a DM automation that sends a link to a public **GitHub Gist** ("five-repos-ai-builders.md"). It is free, with no product, price or affiliate sign visible. The creator positions herself as a builder ("my briefs", "my voice journal"), so this is audience growth. All five links are listed above, so there is no need to engage.

### Verification & insights
- Remotes were checked with `git ls-remote` and clones in scratchpad/repos (omarchy, tobi__qmd, superpowers, i-have-adhd, bilawalsidhu__gods-eye-view): all MIT and all with commits in Oct 2026. Nothing was executed.
- **Number claims are inflated.** Omarchy's 41k is cumulative, not monthly. Superpowers' 287k and i-have-adhd's 47k exceed the latest figures found (about 265k and about 30.7k). "Biggest one this week" mixes all-time totals with weekly trending.
- The gist shown at the end does not match the video's five (it opens with everything-claude-code), which suggests a reused template. The caption's "Three of the five are one person's taste shipped whole as software" is opinion.
- Practical cautions: Omarchy wipes the target drive. God's Eye View mixes live and simulated layers and needs paid or optional API keys for some features.

## DeithvDucpZ | @dripthecup | "10 most powerful GitHub repos you can give Claude" (Microsoft, Anthropic, Stripe and more) plus JetBrains' test of token savers rtk / caveman / ponytail
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DeithvDucpZ/
- **Account:** @dripthecup (Dripthecup) · **Posted:** 2026-10-16 · **Type:** reel (118.2s, animated explainer; the scene detector caught 1 cut, so content was reviewed from all 236 2-fps frames; downloaded via yt-dlp because only the cover image was in the folder)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram)
- **Category:** GitHub & dev
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE (mostly). All ten repos exist and the facts shown check out: licenses, Apify's $5/month free usage, Open SEO's pricing, Expo's $99/yr Apple requirement, and JetBrains' July 2026 measurements of rtk +7.6% cost, caveman 8.5% and ponytail 10.3%. Caveats: Anthropic's claude-security plugin is not open source ("All rights reserved", free to use in Claude Code), the Google Workspace CLI is "not an officially supported Google product", and some tools have paid tiers. The video is unusually honest about costs.

### Post description
> Microsoft, anthropic and stripe all made free github repos that make claude way more powerful 👀
>
> dripthecup.com/links (link in bio)
>
> #claudecode #claude #github #aitools #dripthecup

### Audio
Narration (by the cartoon co-host "Capp"; Whisper misheard "HeyGen" as "Hagen", "Apify's" as "Appafi's", "Addy Osmani… and /build does" as "Adi Osmani… and Bill does", and "Expo's" as "Expose"). Condensed into timestamped notes:
- [0.0–9.3] Hook: "These might be the 10 most powerful GitHub repos you can give Claude," some straight from Microsoft and Anthropic, "plus one with 83,000 stars that made Claude more expensive in a real test."
- [9.3–12.6] "I installed 40 repos this week. I use one…" (counting down to ten worth keeping).
- [12.6–22.4] 1. **HyperFrames** (HeyGen): describe a video and Claude builds it and renders a real MP4 locally; rendering is free; the title card took one request and just over two minutes.
- [22.4–30.4] 2. **Humanizer**: built on Wikipedia's guide to spotting AI writing; in 15 seconds their stiff coffee-shop copy "lost the hype and kept every fact."
- [30.4–37.6] 3. **Google Workspace CLI**: one command-line tool for Gmail, Drive, Calendar, Docs and Sheets, with skills Claude can use.
- [37.6–44.8] 4. **Apify's MCP server**: thousands of ready-made scrapers (e.g. Instagram, Amazon), with **$5 of free usage a month**.
- [44.8–51.3] 5. **Open SEO**: real keyword data, rankings and backlinks so Claude's SEO advice uses real numbers.
- [51.3–59.8] 6. **Agent Skills** by Addy Osmani, "who now works on Claude Code": spec and plan saved as files, /build does the next task, so a new session knows where you are.
- [59.8–65.1] 7. **Expo's official skills** for phone apps, including their list of **20 tells** that make an app look generated.
- [65.1–70.8] 8. **Supabase's and Stripe's own skills**: give Claude their security rules, e.g. users only seeing their own data.
- [70.8–77.4] 9. **Playwright CLI, from Microsoft**: a real browser clicks through your app, and the page map goes to a file instead of Claude's chat.
- [77.4–84.5] 10. **Claude Security**, Anthropic's own plugin: some agents hunt for holes, others try to disprove each, and you review the patches.
- [84.6–101.3] Which cost more? **RTK** advertised **60 to 90% savings**; in JetBrains' July test "it never saved", and at low effort cost **7.6% more**. **Caveman** advertised **65**, JetBrains measured **8.5**. **Ponytail** came out about **10% cheaper**, "Half what it claimed".
- [101.3–109.2] Two tips: **Codeburn** shows where your AI spend goes; **Claude-Mem**'s own docs say its **anonymous telemetry is on by default**, and one command turns it off.
- [109.2–115.1] "39 repos. Gone." Every install line is on dripthecup.com. CTA: "Send this to someone with 40 repos installed."

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"dripthecup.com/links (link in bio)" goes to the creator's own site, which lists every install line and source for free (that page was fetched). The business side is a merch pre-order shop and an optional paid learning group. Nothing in this video is sponsored or affiliate-linked, as far as can be seen. No comment-for-DM gate.

### Verification & insights
- `git ls-remote` and shallow or blobless clones of hyperframes, humanizer, googleworkspace/cli, apify-mcp-server, open-seo, agent-skills, expo/skills, supabase/agent-skills, stripe/ai, playwright-cli, claude-plugins-official, rtk, caveman, ponytail, codeburn and claude-mem. Licenses and READMEs read. No code was run.
- The JetBrains numbers were verified via web search (JetBrains AI blog posts, July 2026; also InfoWorld and others), and the on-screen figures match.
- "Microsoft, Anthropic and Stripe all made free github repos": Microsoft's playwright-cli (Apache-2.0) and Stripe's plugin are open repos. Anthropic's claude-security is free to use but proprietary. Supabase and Expo are also official vendor repos.
- Risks: scrapers (Apify) can violate target sites' ToS. Plugins and skills run with your permissions (the claude-security README itself recommends a sandbox for untrusted repos). Claude-mem telemetry is on by default.
- Could not verify: star counts, Addy Osmani's current employer, Open SEO's $0.50 trial, and the "40 repos installed" anecdote.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- **"Comment repo"** gets a free DM list of 20+ repos (a lead magnet for follower growth and DM automation).
- **"Comment learn"** leads to the creator's **course community** ("build AI agents and make money from them"), almost certainly a paid Skool-style group. Web search found nothing on @raycfu, so the name, platform and price could not be identified.

### Verification & insights
- All 10 repos were confirmed via `git ls-remote` and shallow clones in scratchpad/repos (superpowers, awesome-claude-code and everything-claude-code reused existing clones). LICENSE and last commit are as in the table. Nothing was executed.
- **Licensing caveats:** Claude Squad is AGPL-3.0 (copyleft, which matters if modified and offered as a network service). awesome-claude-code is CC BY-NC-ND (fine to read; not reusable commercially or in modified form).
- **"I tested all of them" (thousands of repos):** implausible hype.
- **"Karpathy Claude skills file":** an unaffiliated community repo inspired by Karpathy, not his work.
- **everything-claude-code "built from 10 months of real usage… most complete":** self-description. The repo has grown since (it now has more skills), so the video's numbers are a snapshot.
- **Not verified:** the creator's identity, upload date and course price.

### Post description
> Spacedrive flips the script on how we manage files. Built in Rust with a TypeScript front‑end, this open‑source project lets you merge local disks, NAS, and cloud buckets into a single, searchable virtual filesystem. It maintains a live index for instant folder loads, offers full‑history snapshots, and even lets you preview file operations before they happen. Share specific folders securely, mount remote sources, or automate tasks via its powerful CLI—all without any vendor lock‑in or telemetry. With 39,178 stars and a thriving community, it's a solid foundation for developers seeking a self‑hosted, extensible storage layer. Dive into the repo, try the nightly builds, and take back control of your data.
>
>  #Spacedrive #OpenSource #RustLang #FileSystem #SelfHosted #DeveloperTools #DataOwnership #GitHub #CLI #CrossPlatform #Privacy #TechCommunity

### Audio

- [9.1–12.6] "With over 39K stars it already has a solid community."
- [12.6–19.6] "It builds a live index, so folders open instantly without rescanning, and you can preview any operation before it runs."
- [19.6–27.3] "Share a folder with a friend, mount remote sources, or run the CLI for automated scripts all without handing your data to a third-party service."
- [27.3–34.3] "Think of it as your personal cloud that you fully control, with history, duplicate detection and end-to-end encryption built in."
- [34.3–37.9] "Perfect for developers who want a self-hosted, extensible storage layer."

### On-screen content, page by page
**Single page (0.0–38.1s):** A browser mock-up titled "meow.codes" at the URL `https://github.com/spacedriveapp/spacedrive`, scrolling top to bottom through the README. Burned-in captions repeat the narration. Sections shown, in order (I checked them against the cloned README):
- Logo and "Spacedrive — Your data should not have to live on somebody else's computer to be useful." Badges: License Apache-2.0, Core Rust + TypeScript, Discord (members online). Links: Download · Docs · Discord · Build from source.

The beta "commits to Spacedrive's data model and to preserving your library … brings portable source indexes, private remote access, mounts, history, extensions, and one API for people and software." Nightly CLI and daemon builds are "unsigned, move with every push to main, and can break".

- Sections: "Own where your data lives", "One filesystem, every source", "Protection is part of the filesystem", "See every change before it happens", "Access anything from anywhere", "One API for people and software", "Extend the filesystem", "Architecture" (Mermaid flowchart text), "Install a nightly" (shasum and `sd daemon install` commands, `sd config set update.channel nightly`), "Build from source" (Rust 1.95 or newer, Bun 1.3.4, Node 20, `just`), "Project history", "Contributing", "License: Apache-2.0. Releases before 2026-03-24 were published under AGPL-3.0 and remain available under that license."

Peer connections use Iroh and QUIC with end-to-end encryption.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No call to action, no comment keyword and no product. The account showcases GitHub repos, apparently for engagement. Nothing is sold.

### Verification & insights
- The narration's claims match the README, which is what the video scrolls through. They are the project's own marketing, not independent tests.
- **Maturity:** 2.0 has not shipped. Nightly builds are explicitly unsigned and "can break", and the README advises keeping backups of any library you point them at. Treat it as beta software, not a "solid foundation" for important data yet.
- **Licence:** Apache-2.0 since 2026-03-24 (earlier releases AGPL-3.0). The repo has CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md files, so AI-assisted development is in use.
- **Could not verify:** the exact star count, or any of the performance and security claims.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment RUFLO" is an Instagram auto-DM, and the frames show it sends "Here's the GitHub link". The link is simply `github.com/ruvnet/ruflo`. No paid product appears. The end card asks viewers to follow for "More Claude Code mods".

### Verification & insights
- The install steps and the console screens are genuine Ruflo; the README matches the screen word for word.
- **Security caveats from Ruflo's own README:**
  - "Mods run with your account's permissions; review their code before installing."
  - Installing these plugins added 3 agents, 9 hooks and 1 MCP server to the creator's setup (the before and after reload counts above).
  - The federation features talk to external relay servers run by the author (relay.ruv.io).
- **Claims vs reality:** The "100+ specialized agents", "self-learning memory" and "enterprise security guardrails" claims are vendor claims. The README itself footnotes that the learning loop is "conceptual". Swarm features also multiply token use and cost.
- The "2 errors during load" and the ignored hook "description" keys on screen show the setup was not fully clean.
- **Could not verify:** the star count, the full caption and the post date.

### Verification & insights
- **Repo:** clone made and README read. The licence is MIT and the on-screen news items match the README. The newest entry (EAGLE-4) was added after the video was recorded.
- **Claims vs reality:**

- **Trading angle:** The caption's link to trading latency is a stretch. TokenSpeed matters only if you self-host large models on datacenter GPUs, not for retail trading tools or exchange-latency work.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment 7 and I'll send you all 7 links… Make sure you're following so you actually see my DM" is a comment-to-DM automation for engagement and follower growth. The links are just the 7 free GitHub repos above. The account brands itself "AI for Personal Brands". I could not confirm whether the creator sells a community or course (web search found nothing about him); no price or affiliate sign appears in the post.

### Verification & insights
- Shallow clones in scratchpad/repos: browser-use__browser-use, rohitg00__agentmemory, K-Dense-AI__scientific-agent-skills, cathrynlavery__diagram-design, volcengine__OpenViking, mukul975__Anthropic-Cybersecurity-Skills (existing), awesome-harness-engineering (existing). I read the LICENSE files, READMEs and last commits; nothing was executed.
- The naming trap: "Anthropic Cybersecurity Skills" sounds official but isn't. Treat third-party security skills, which tell an agent to run tools, with care.
- Licence trap: OpenViking's AGPLv3 core matters if you embed it in a product or network service.
- "Months of building" and "nobody is talking about them" are marketing; several of these repos have tens of thousands of stars.

- [0.0–4.0] "AICodeKing just solved the biggest headache with AI context amnesia."
- [4.0–8.0] "Every time you start a new session, your AI models forget your entire context."
- [8.0–12.0] "Meet leanrea. It connects directly into your developer workflow."
- [12.0–17.0] "It stores persistent long-term memory, so your AI coding agents remember your entire workflow."
- [17.0–20.0] "It hands control right back to you with zero friction."
- [20.0–24.0] "It is 100% free and open source with over 641 stars."
- [24.0–29.0] "Comment skill below, and I will DM you the direct repo link and setup guide instantly."
- [30.0–34.0] "Thank you for watching!" (probably a Whisper artifact at the end)

### On-screen content, page by page
There is one templated page for the whole 30s. A header reads **"leanrea / One 8 KB skill that replaces REA (Reverse Engineer…"** with badges "⭐ 641 Stars · Developer Tool · AI Memory Layer". Below it is a window titled "🔒 LEANREA · OPEN SOURCE" that scrolls slowly through the GitHub README. A footer CTA reads "👇 Comment "SKILL" · Direct GitHub Link + Setup" (near the end: "💬 Comment "SKILL" for Direct Repo Link + Setup"). Captions over the window echo the voiceover ("YOUR ENTIRE CONTEXT", "IT STORES PERSISTENT LONG-TERM").

The README content as scrolled. I checked it against the cloned repo, and it matches exactly.
- **Tagline:** "One 8 KB skill file that does what REA does: no MCP server, no 207 tools, no setup." REA (Reverse Engineer Anything) puts an MCP layer between the agent and tools it can already use: asar, otool, nm, `go version -m`, jadx, apktool, Ghidra, tshark, binwalk, sqlite3. leanrea drops that layer, tells the agent which command is fastest, gives recon commands and lists traps.
- **Comparison table (REA 6.5.0 vs leanrea):**
  - Installed: 25 MB npm package + MCP server vs one `SKILL.md`
  - Instructions: 23.5 KB skill + 48.8 KB references vs 8 KB
  - Tools added: 207 (3.35 MB of definitions) vs 0 (uses Bash)
  - Context for "Reply OK" with all tools loaded: 400.7k tokens, $1.60 vs 34.5k tokens, $0.09
- **Benchmark setup:** Claude Code headless (`claude -p`), Sonnet 5.5, macOS arm64. 4 reverse-engineering tasks × 3 reps × 3 arms, graded against hand-made keys.

  | arm | wall time | cost | turns | tokens | score |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | leanrea | 388 s | $2.17 | 55 | 2.40 M | 74/75 |
  | no skill | 479 s | $2.21 | 81 | 3.11 M | 67/75 (one refusal) |
  | REA (MCP + its skill) | 627 s | $3.36 | 124 | 5.93 M | 75/75 |

  "Against REA, leanrea had 38% less wall time, 35% lower cost, 56% fewer turns and 60% fewer tokens, at the same accuracy." Its one partial miss named an older IPC channel.
- **Tasks:**
  - electron: trace a feature through app.asar
  - native: exports, TCC permission check, single-instance lock of a `.node` addon
  - go: toolchain and dependency versions of a stripped `gh` binary
  - deep: socket path format, wire protocol and peer auth, from disassembly alone
- **Operation timings (REA vs raw):**
  - Go build info: 0.56 s vs `go version -m` 0.06 s
  - Mach-O overview: 3.32 s vs otool + dyld_info + nm 0.04 s
  - Electron analysis: REA fails on asar integrity until a flag is passed, then takes 47.9 s and 140 MB of JSON; raw asar extract + `rg -uu` takes 4.8 s and 24 KB
- **Caveats:**
  - Only 3 reps per cell, one model, one machine.
  - REA was tested in its best case (deferred tool loading).
  - Ghidra, Hopper and jadx weren't installed.
  - leanrea's gain over no skill is only about 19% faster and 32% fewer turns.
- **Install:**
  - `npx skills add aicodeking/leanrea`, with variants `-g`, `-a claude-code -y`, `-g -a '*' -y`, and `npx skills update leanrea`
  - Manual: `git clone https://github.com/aicodeking/leanrea` and copy into `~/.claude/skills/`
  - Usage: `/leanrea How does the search feature in Foo.app work? Show the evidence.`
- **"What's in the skill":**
  - Rules: work on a copy; route by `file`/magic bytes; slice minified files; batch probes; `rg -uu`; mark claims seen/inferred/unknown
  - Router by target type: Electron/asar, minified bundles, Hermes/bytenode, Mach-O, ELF/PE, Go/Rust/.NET/APK/Flutter, WASM/SQLite/plists/pcap/firmware, websites
  - "First moves"
  - "Speed patterns" (cut off by the end of the scroll)

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| github.com/aicodeking/leanrea | repo | caption, page | Real. MIT License, © 2026 AICodeKing. Last commit 09ba4aa, 2026-10-17 ("docs: simplify usage example"). `leanrea/SKILL.md` is 8,374 bytes. About 1.2k stars and 178 forks on the GitHub page when checked. |
| AICodeKing | creator | caption, audio | The repo owner, who also runs a known AI-coding YouTube channel. Not the poster. |
| REA (Reverse Engineer Anything), morluto/rea, npm `rea-agents@6.5.0` | repo/tool | page | The project leanrea benchmarks against. leanrea says it is not affiliated with REA. |
| "591 Stars" / "641 stars" | number | caption / audio + page | Two counts, probably taken on different days. The repo now has about 1.2k. |
| "AI Memory Layer" / "persistent long-term memory" | claim | page badge, audio | False. Nothing in the README or skill concerns memory or context persistence. |
| skills CLI (skills.sh) | tool | page | The installer the README uses (`npx skills add`). |
| Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI, OpenCode | apps | page | Agents the skills CLI can install into. |
| Sonnet 5.5 | model | page | The model used in the benchmark. |
| "SKILL" | CTA keyword | audio, page | Comment-to-DM trigger. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment SKILL" sends a DM with the GitHub link and a "setup guide". The link is already in the caption and on screen: github.com/aicodeking/leanrea, which is free. @repopicks appears to be a repo-aggregator page that grows engagement through comment-to-DM automation. No product or price was shown. The truncated "📩 Get the 5 ..." probably promotes a further freebie or list; it could not be retrieved.

### Verification & insights
- I ran `git ls-remote` and a shallow clone (scratchpad/repos/aicodeking__leanrea) and read the LICENSE (MIT), the README and the latest commit. The on-screen README is a faithful copy. I did not run the benchmark scripts (no-execution rule).
- **Main problem:** the narration is generic template copy that does not match the repo. leanrea is a reverse-engineering helper (inspecting Electron apps, Mach-O, Go binaries, APKs and so on). It does not give agents memory. Anyone looking for an "AI memory" tool would be misled.
- **Benchmark claims:** they are the author's own and small-sample (3 reps, one model, one machine), and the README states this itself. The "38% / 35% / 56% / 60%" figures match the table arithmetic (388 vs 627 s gives −38%; $2.17 vs $3.36 gives −35%; 55 vs 124 turns gives −56%; 2.40M vs 5.93M tokens gives −60%).
- The skill is plain text. It tells an agent to run local CLI tools on binaries you point it at, so the usual care applies when analysing untrusted binaries.
- **Not verified:** the full caption, @repopicks' display name and bio link.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Only "Follow for more @techskills" and the FOMO slide "You won't see this page again". There is no comment keyword, link-in-bio product or price, so this is follower growth. Indirect commercial interests sit inside the promoted repos themselves: MuAPI paid API and white-label plans (Open Generative AI), and Fincept's paid Enterprise/Pro/Quantcept tiers.

### Verification & insights
- All six repos were checked with `git ls-remote` and cloned shallow (or reused) in scratchpad/repos. LICENSE, README and the last commit were read; no code was run. All six are genuine and actively maintained as of October 2026.
- **"No subscriptions, no API bills" (Open-Gen-AI) is misleading.** Generation mainly goes through MuAPI's pay-per-use API; only some models run locally in the desktop app, which needs capable hardware. The "no content filters" positioning also carries legal and ToS risk if you sell outputs.
- **"Save $24,000/year"**: Fincept's own README compares itself with about $27,000 for a Bloomberg seat. But the free AGPL edition relies on free public feeds plus your own API keys. It is not real-time institutional data and not a Bloomberg replacement for professional use. AGPL-3.0 also imposes source-sharing obligations if you offer it as a service to clients.
- **"Charge businesses $1,500+ per audit"** is an invented price. The tool's output depends on the account data you feed it, and agency pricing depends on expertise, not the tool.
- **Camofox "Avoid detection" / "Scrape websites"**: it is explicitly built to evade bot detection (Cloudflare, Google) and import login cookies. That can breach website terms of service (for example LinkedIn and Amazon prohibit scraping) and anti-circumvention or computer-misuse laws depending on use. This is legal and ToS risk if sold as a service.
- **Vibe Trading "Sell custom trading systems"**: selling trading systems or signals to others can trigger financial-advice and adviser registration rules in many jurisdictions. Backtests made by LLM agents are not evidence of profitability.
- The "GITHUB TRENDING #1 Repository Of The Day" badge on Vibe-Trading is a README image and was not independently verified. Star counts could not be confirmed because the GitHub API was not available in this session.
- Related: TencentDB Agent Memory (DdKExW5t68N) shows the same "#1 Repository of the Day" badge style, and Vibe-Trading also appears among the existing clones in scratchpad/repos from earlier batches.

| "AI" | comment keyword | caption, slide 7 | DM link to "the full list of free AI resources", "AI gems daily" and "Claude's full course". |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Comment "AI" leads to an automated DM with a link to a resource list, a "Claude's full course" lead magnet and daily AI content (most likely a newsletter or link page that collects emails; an earlier post by the same account, DegkHS3GRHJ, ran the identical "Claude's full course" funnel). No price is shown. The repos themselves need no comment: all five are public on GitHub (links in the table above).

### Verification & insights
- **Checked:** `git ls-remote` plus shallow clones into `scratchpad/repos/` for The-Swarm-Corporation__AutoHedge, Fincept-Corporation__FinceptTerminal, danny-avila__LibreChat and ahujasid__blender-mcp (Vibe-Trading was already cloned). LICENSE, README and `git log -1` were read for each. Nothing was executed.
- **"100% free & zero cost":** partly true. The code is open source, but Fincept and MCP for Blender have paid upsells, and every tool needs paid LLM APIs (or local models) to be useful.
- **AutoHedge "skips years of learning… live on Solana":** dangerous framing. It is an LLM-agent bot that trades crypto from a hot wallet whose private key you paste into an env file. It has no published track record, and the repo has been quiet since March 2026. Treat it as a demo, not a hedge fund. The author's Swarms ecosystem is also associated with a Solana token, which is worth knowing when judging "hedge fund" marketing.
- **Vibe-Trading:** the strongest of the five for the user's interests (an active research lab project with backtests, an alpha library and broker connectors). Its own changelog shows ongoing look-ahead fixes (e.g. a momentum factor that "read today's price"), which is a reminder to validate its outputs.
- **"Feels illegal to know":** clickbait. These are popular, trending repos.
- **Not verified:** the contents of the DM resource list and "course".

## DdrPPALI9iA | @justinmendez.ai | NVIDIA Model Optimizer (ModelOpt) repo pitch: "quantization, pruning…", plus a claim of running Qwen3.8-27B on a laptop for near-zero coding costs
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DdrPPALI9iA/
- **Account:** @justinmendez.ai (Justin Mendez) · **Posted:** 2026-09-24 · **Type:** reel (59.1s, 1 static page: the creator green-screened over the GitHub README)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram, fetched with yt-dlp)
- **Category:** GitHub & dev
- **Verdict:** PARTLY TRUE. The repo is real, free and Apache-2.0 (NVIDIA/Model-Optimizer, actively maintained). But it is a toolkit for quantizing models for NVIDIA-GPU inference stacks (TensorRT-LLM, vLLM, SGLang). It is not a way to run big models on a laptop "without having to buy a GPU". The "billions of tokens a month on my laptop" claim is not credible for a 27B dense model on a laptop.

### Post description
> Nvidia model optimizer repo is a gold mine if you’re getting into hosting and deploying lambs or want to play around with local source models, this will really help you be able to run much larger models on your local hardware without having to buy GPU user anything else this is how I run Qwen 3.8 27b on my laptop #llms #ai #opensourcesoftware

(The text is verbatim. "lambs" is speech-to-text for "LLMs" and "GPU user" for "GPUs or".)

### Audio
The creator speaks in English, overlaid on the README.
- [0.0–4.1] "If you're running actual LLMs, I think you should check this out."
- [4.1–13.7] "So NVIDIA's model optimizer here, this is an open source repo that gives you all the tools and their own internal testing and benchmarks around how to optimize a model."
- [13.7–20.3] "So it's like making the model smaller, but not just making the model smaller, but making the model faster. You'd think those two are connected, but they're not."
- [20.3–25.3] "So this is like quantization, pruning, all kinds of [expletive] in here."
- [25.3–36.0] "If you're really wanting to get into actually building LLMs, I highly recommend you read through this and use it because … I use quantization [garbled: 'struggle in this morning']."
- [36.0–52.7] "I use it to be able to run Qwen 3.8 on my laptop, right? I have that running locally, the 27 billion parameter model just on my laptop and it's cut down my coding costs to near zero, pushing billions of tokens a month."
- [52.7–58.9] "So highly recommend you try it out. Link's going to be in the comments below. You don't have to comment anything."

### On-screen content, page by page
**Page 1 (0.0–59.1s, the only scene):** The GitHub README of **NVIDIA Model Optimizer** in dark mode, with tabs README / Code of conduct / More, and a green banner of technique icons (Quantization, Pruning, Distillation, Speculative Decoding, Sparsity). The badges read **Documentation: latest**, **Release: v0.47.0** and **License: Apache 2.0**, followed by the links Documentation | Roadmap | Announcement Blogs. The visible README text says ModelOpt is a library of model optimization techniques: quantization, pruning, Neural Architecture Search (NAS), distillation, speculative decoding and sparsity. **[Input]:** Hugging Face, PyTorch or ONNX models. **[Optimize]:** Python APIs to compose techniques and export an optimized quantized checkpoint; integrated with NVIDIA Megatron-Bridge, Megatron-LM and Hugging Face Accelerate. A cut-out of the creator covers the middle, with burned-in word-by-word subtitles.

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| NVIDIA Model Optimizer (ModelOpt), github.com/NVIDIA/Model-Optimizer | repo | p1, audio 4s, caption | Real. Apache-2.0. Last commit 92a3cd5 on 2026-10-20. Tags up to 0.48.0rc1 (the video shows release v0.47.0). PyPI package `nvidia-modelopt`. Formerly "TensorRT Model Optimizer", renamed Dec 2025. Exports quantized checkpoints (FP8, NVFP4, INT4 and others) for SGLang, TensorRT-LLM, vLLM and similar. |
| Quantization, pruning, NAS, distillation, speculative decoding, sparsity | techniques | p1, audio 20s | Accurately listed from the README |
| Qwen 3.8 27B (Qwen3.8-27B) | model | audio 36–42s, caption | Real: Alibaba's dense 27B open-weights (Apache-2.0) multimodal model, released 2026-08-14. Reports say quantized builds of about 9–17 GB run on a 16 GB GPU or Mac via LM Studio or llama.cpp. |
| "Link's going to be in the comments" | CTA | audio 55s | A pinned comment with the repo link; no keyword gate ("you don't have to comment anything") |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Nothing is sold. The link in the comments is the free GitHub repo. This is engagement or authority content; no product, price or affiliate sign appears in the post. I could not identify the creator's bio link (web search found no clear profile for "justinmendez.ai").

### Verification & insights
- I shallow-cloned NVIDIA/Model-Optimizer into scratchpad/repos/NVIDIA__Model-Optimizer and read the LICENSE (Apache 2.0), README and latest commit. Nothing was executed.
- **"Run much larger models on your local hardware without having to buy GPU":** misleading. ModelOpt's quantization and export pipeline is built around NVIDIA GPUs (CUDA calibration, TensorRT-LLM, vLLM and SGLang, and NVFP4/FP8 formats for Hopper and Blackwell). Laptop users without an NVIDIA GPU (for example on a Mac) would normally run community GGUF or MLX quantizations through llama.cpp, LM Studio or Ollama, which need no ModelOpt at all. Quantization does let a 27B model fit in about 16 GB, so the general idea is true.
- **"Smaller but not just smaller, faster":** broadly fair. ModelOpt publishes throughput gains (for example its README cites up to 1.30x vLLM throughput for Qwen3.6-35B-A3B NVFP4, and 2.6x for a pruned and distilled Nemotron Nano).
- **"Billions of tokens a month… coding costs near zero":** implausible on a laptop. One billion tokens a month is about 385 tokens/s sustained 24/7. A 27B dense model on laptop hardware typically generates tens of tokens per second (prompt processing is faster, but this is still a stretch). Electricity and hardware cost are ignored, and local 27B quality differs from frontier hosted models despite Alibaba's self-reported benchmarks.
- The creator does not name his laptop's hardware, the quantization format or the inference runtime, so the setup cannot be reproduced from the post.

Per its README, Twitter needs cookies exported with Cookie-Editor (`TWITTER_AUTH_TOKEN`, `TWITTER_CT0`). ### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "Like this video and comment REPOS" is engagement bait plus an auto-DM with the GitHub link (given above). Nothing is sold in the post.
- Note: the Agent Reach README itself carries sponsor/affiliate blocks for scraping services (BrowserAct, CoreClaw with UTM referral links). The project is free, but its README advertises paid scrapers.

### Verification & insights
- **Clone checked** (`scratchpad/repos/Agent-Reach`): MIT. Docs in Chinese plus README_en/ja/ko. The README says "完全免费 — 所有工具开源、所有 API 免费。唯一可能花钱的是服务器代理（$1/月），本地电脑不需要" (completely free; the only possible cost is a server proxy at about $1/month, not needed on a local computer).
- **"Zero API keys" is technically true and practically misleading.** Instead of keys, several platforms need your **session cookies** or logged-in browser, which are as sensitive as passwords. The README warns (⚠️ 封号风险提醒) that cookie-based scripted access risks having the account **detected and banned**, and recommends a throwaway account.
- **"Generate leads… even LinkedIn":** LinkedIn's User Agreement prohibits scraping and automated access, and accounts get restricted. Mass lead scraping also raises GDPR and privacy issues. This is the riskiest part of the pitch.
- **"Close to 100,000 stars":** the screenshot shows 97.5k. Plausible for a fast-growing repo, but not independently confirmed today.
- **The caption is more honest than the audio:** "free doesn't mean access free… session cookies are basically passwords. check what the installer adds before you run it." That advice is sound.
- Not executed. The installer was not run.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment stack and find the link in the comments" leads to the free GitHub repo (engagement play for the creator). The creator sells nothing visible. The repo itself has a paid upsell, ECC Pro, from $19 per seat per month for private repos, plus GitHub Sponsors. Direct link: github.com/affaan-m/ECC.

### Verification & insights
- Shallow clone: LICENSE (MIT) and README read; repo docs grepped for the hackathon, tests and stars claims. No code was run.
- The README itself warns: "Official sources only… Third-party re-uploads and unofficial mirrors… may contain malware". Install only from github.com/affaan-m/ECC, ecc.tools, or the npm packages it names.
- The README also warns: "Do not stack install methods". Installing twice duplicates skills, hooks and configs. Installing hundreds of skills, agents and hooks adds context overhead and hooks that run on your machine. Review hooks before enabling them.
- "Transforms Claude Code into a full engineering team instantly" / "battle-tested": marketing. The tests figure seems to belong to one component.
- The presenter and B-roll are stock-style footage with heavy effects. Nothing shown is a real measured outcome.

- I confirmed the site and its structure from the screen and my general knowledge. All stats above are as displayed on Bulkowski's pages.
- **Claim "it gives you the success rate as a percentage":** partly true. The site mostly gives failure rates, average rise/decline, throwback rates and target-hit rates, measured on historical (mostly US stock, older) samples. These are descriptive statistics. They are not a tested strategy with costs, slippage or out-of-sample checks.
- **Claim "everything you need to know before investing a single dollar":** an overstatement. It is a useful reference, but pattern identification is subjective, and many pages were last revised years ago (e.g. Busted Patterns 12/29/2020, Event Patterns latest release 2010).

## DepJxXTRCTR | @janus_labs | "Building Janus, Episode 2: How the Bot Fits Together", an animated architecture diagram of a trading bot (data adapter, order book, strategy, order controller, risk checks, money manager/ledger, execution adapter, supervisor) with a tiny Python authorize() example
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DepJxXTRCTR/
- **Account:** @janus_labs (Janus Labs) · **Posted:** 2026-10-18 · **Type:** reel (133s, one continuously animated diagram; checked through the 2-fps frames, since scene detection found only 1 page)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram, refetched with yt-dlp)
- **Category:** Trading strategy (bot engineering / architecture)
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE (educational). A sound, standard separation-of-duties design for an order-management system. It makes no profit claims. The "link in bio" offering could not be identified.

### Post description
> Some housekeeping before we get started; a bot needs clear responsibilities. Who proposes a trade, who approves it, and who manages the money.
>
> Building a bit that can place trades is one thing, building one that can manage our cash and inventory correctly is another. Let's do it right from day one.
>
> #tradingbot #algotrading #riskmanagment #trading #tradingreels

("a bit" is the creator's typo for "a bot".) At the time of posting: 30 likes, 0 comments.

### Audio
Calm English voice-over, possibly synthetic, with no music detected. The speech runs about 330 words; timestamped notes, with key lines quoted:
- [0.0–9.6] Hook: "A trading bot needs a brain. Why shouldn't that brain be allowed to move the money directly?" Title: Building Janus, Episode 2, how the bot fits together.
- [9.6–24.6] Recaps that episode 1 showed why an edge matters. Each piece needs clear responsibilities; otherwise a correct calculation can still become an unsafe order or a wrong account balance.
- [24.6–41.4] Market data: an adapter converts a platform's messages into a consistent internal format; a local order book holds current bids, asks and available quantities, plus the information needed to judge whether that state is usable.
- [38.4–47.4] A strategy reads that state and proposes an action. "Its proposal is an intent, not permission to trade." The controller coordinates what happens next.
- [47.4–55.1] The controller asks risk whether the data and exposure are acceptable, and the money manager whether the needed cash or inventory can be reserved.
- [55.1–65.6] "Only an authorized, reserved intent reaches the execution adapter," which translates the request for the venue without changing the strategy or deciding to spend more.
- [65.6–79.3] The order controller tracks the order life cycle; the ledger applies actual fills to cash and inventory; a supervisory process compares internal state with the external account and can stop new orders on any disagreement.
- [79.3–97.6] The code example: one function proposes a small buy from a quote; a separate function checks its cost against available cash and a pause flag. He notes it is not a complete exchange connection.
- [97.6–119.2] The pieces can be called services or modules and need not all be independent AI agents; giving several agents separate authority over the same cash "would create the very coordination problem we need to solve." "Janus needs one coherent record of commitments and one controlled route for orders."
- [119.2–132.8] Call to action: download the code examples and skills via the link in bio. Next episode starts on the research engine and why the balance can swing more even as an average advantage becomes clearer.

### On-screen content, page by page
A dark-themed animated diagram that builds up step by step. Times are approximate, from the 2-fps frames.
**0–4s:** A circle "STRATEGY – the brain" and a box "$ – the account". A red ✕ appears between them, with the text "move the money **directly?**"
**~8–10s:** Title card "BUILDING JANUS · EPISODE 2 · How the Bot Fits Together".
**~12–25s:** A faint outline of all the boxes, captioned "**clear responsibilities**". Two red tags flash briefly (an unsafe order and a wrong balance).
**~25–45s:** The diagram builds from the top. "VENUE · external platform & account" (dashed box, with "market data" on the left and "orders · fills" on the right) → "**Data adapter** – many formats → one" → "**Local order book** – ask $2.00 · bids · sizes", with green badges "fresh ✓" and "in order ✓" → "**Strategy** – reads state · proposes", which emits an intent tag "buy 10 @ $2.00".
**~47–55s:** "**Order controller**" holding the intent "buy 10 @ $2.00". "**Risk checks** – data fresh ✓, exposure OK ✓". "**Account record**", with the money manager showing **available $60 · reserved $20** and the ledger showing **cash $80 · units 0**.
**~55–65s:** "**Execution adapter** – translates only · no new decisions". The order goes to the venue as "BUY 10 2.00 LMT" ("venue format"). Order lifecycle pills: proposed › authorised › **sent** › filled.
**~65–73s:** Legend: "data / intent / order / confirmation". A confirmation comes back as "FILLED 10 @ 2.00" ("paper fill · illustrative"). The lifecycle reaches "filled".
**~73–79s:** The ledger now shows **cash $60 · units 10**, with available $60 · reserved $0. "**Supervisor** – ours $60 · 10 units = venue ✓".
**~80–96s:** "this lesson's boundary — the rest is introduced later" (chips: data adapter, order book, risk checks, money manager, execution, ledger, supervisor). Flow: quote `{"ask": 2.0}` → Strategy `propose(quote)` → intent `side: "buy", qty: 10, limit: 2.0`. Then Order controller `authorize(intent, 80.0, False)`: "cost = 10 × 2.0 = 20.0", "20.0 <= 80.0 ✓ not paused ✓" → "allowed = True", tag "paper input · no exchange". Output: `{'side': 'buy', 'qty': 10, 'limit': 2.0} True`.
  The code panel is "public-example.py" (Python · paper input), 11 short lines. Excerpt:
  ```
  def propose(quote):
      return {"side": "buy", "qty": 10, "limit": quote["ask"]}
  def authorize(intent, available_cash, paused):
      cost = intent["qty"] * intent["limit"]
      return not paused and cost <= available_cash
  ```
  The remaining lines set `quote = {"ask": 2.0}`, call both functions with available_cash=80.0 and paused=False, and print the intent and the decision.
**~98–100s:** "services or modules".
**~100–110s:** "services or modules – not necessarily independent AI agents". A bar labelled "one account" runs from $0 to $80. Agent A "sees $80 → spends $50 ✓ $30 left". Agent B "also saw $80 → spends $50 ✕ overdrawn −$20". Agent C "asks for $50 ✕ no cash left". "balance −$20". A red pill reads "**coordination problem** – no single record of what's already committed".
**~112–122s:** The full diagram returns, captioned "**one record of commitments** / **one controlled route for orders**".
**~124s:** Orange pill "Code + skills · link in bio".
**~128–133s:** "NEXT · EPISODE 3 – **How an Edge Emerges from Luck**".

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- The only call to action is "download the code examples and skills through the link in the bio". No price, product name, course or comment keyword appears. The account is small (30 likes). The bio link could not be fetched (HTTP 429), and web searches for "Janus Labs" with trading or "Building Janus" found nothing. **Undetermined.** It may be a free download, a newsletter or email capture, or a paid pack. "Skills" probably means AI-agent (Claude Code-style) skill files.

### Verification & insights
- The architecture is textbook and correct. It mirrors how real order-management and execution systems are built: a market-data normalizer, a book with staleness and sequence checks ("fresh", "in order"), strategy output as an *intent*, pre-trade risk checks, cash/inventory **reservation** before sending, a dumb execution gateway, an order state machine (proposed → authorised → sent → filled), a fill-driven ledger, and reconciliation with a kill switch (the supervisor).
- The advice that the pieces need not be separate AI agents, and that several agents must not hold independent authority over the same cash, is a valid warning against the "multi-agent trading team" hype seen in other saved posts (e.g. TradingAgents-style repos).
- The code is deliberately minimal. It has no partial fills, fees, rounding or concurrency, as the narrator says himself: "It is not a complete exchange connection."
- **Not verified:** what the bio link contains; who is behind Janus Labs. No GitHub repository or website was found by search.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- CTA: "Comment trading and I'll send both course links." This is a comment-to-DM engagement bait. The DM would simply contain the two public URLs, which are **cmegroup.com/education/courses/introduction-to-futures.html** and **ibkrcampus.com** (Traders' Academy section). No product, price, affiliate code or bio link was found for @nishantaqc. Interactive Brokers runs a referral program, so an IBKR referral link in the DM is possible, but there was no sign of one in the post.

### Verification & insights
- Verified by web search: the CME course exists, is free and is aimed at beginners. IBKR Campus is free to clients and non-clients (per IBKR's 2023 press release). "You don't even need an account" is broadly true for browsing lessons. A free IBKR Campus login may be needed for some features (progress, certain quizzes). Whether quizzes are login-gated today was not confirmed.
- "Beginner to advanced": true for IBKR Traders' Academy, which tags courses by level. The CME intro course itself is beginner-level only; CME has other intermediate courses.
- Note that both are vendor education. CME teaches futures to grow exchange volume, and IBKR's courses lean on its own platform (TWS). Both are factual and non-hype, but neither teaches a profitable strategy.
- The "fake gurus with rented Lambos" framing is the caption's opinion, and the price comparison is invented. The core recommendation is sound.
- Other well-known free official sources: CBOE Options Institute, OCC's Options Education Program, and Investor.gov.
- Not verified: the full caption (429), and whatever the DM actually sends.

## DeqbM5Cs1TB | @doubazcapital | Animated beginner lesson on the DOM (depth of market) using an illustrative NQ ladder: bids, asks, spread, market vs limit orders
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DeqbM5Cs1TB/
- **Account:** @doubazcapital (DOUBAZ CAPITAL | Futures Trading Desk) · **Posted:** 2026-10-19 · **Type:** reel (105.1s, about 20 animated steps; scene detection caught 4 pages, so the 2-fps frames were reviewed)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram)
- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE. It is a correct, basic explanation of order-book mechanics. The NQ tick value ($5 per 0.25 point) is accurate. No product is pitched in this post.

### Post description
Over 150 words; opening lines quoted, the rest condensed.
> Every candle tells a story about executed trades. The DOM shows you the liquidity behind the next move. 📊
>
> DOM = Depth of Market:
> 📍 Prices in the middle, one tick apart (NQ: 0.25 pt = $5)

- Layout: bids (buyers waiting) on the left, asks (sellers waiting) on the right; at the best bid/ask, market buys pay the ask and market sells hit the bid.
- How price moves: market buys execute against resting asks; once the asks at one price are used up, the next ask may be higher, but the book can refill and orders can vanish.
- Key line: "Price only moves when aggression beats the book." Limit orders rest in the book and market orders take them.
- The book doesn't show everything: orders get pulled and some size stays hidden (promised for "Ep 15").
- Billed as Episode 1 of the Order Flow series, next "What Is Order Flow?"; also labelled "DOUBAZ Academy · Episode 12" with "Save this and follow for Episode 13".
- Disclaimer: educational content only, not financial advice; futures carry substantial risk of loss; past examples don't guarantee future results.
- Hashtags: #dom #depthofmarket #orderflow #orderbook #daytrading, plus unhashed futurestrading, nqfutures, scalping, tradingeducation, doubazcapital.

*Caption condensed – full text is on the original post.*

(The numbering is inconsistent: "Episode 1 of our Order Flow series", "DOUBAZ Academy · Episode 12", "follow for Episode 13", and "Ep 15" for hidden size.)

### Audio
English voiceover, condensed into timestamped notes with short quotes.
- [0.0–12.4] Hook: "Every candle tells a story about executed trades." The DOM helps you see the liquidity behind the next price move.
- [12.4–24.4] DOM means depth of market: prices in the middle, buyers waiting on the left, sellers on the right; each number is how many contracts are waiting at that price.
- [24.4–41.4] The highest buyer is the best bid, the lowest seller the best ask, and the gap is the spread (here one tick). A market buy pays the ask; a market sell hits the bid.
- [41.4–55.4] Market buys execute against resting asks; when the asks at one price are consumed, the next ask may be higher, but the book can refill and orders can disappear.
- [55.4–69.4] Limit orders wait in the book (the visible numbers); market orders take them immediately. "Price only moves when aggressive orders eat through what is waiting."
- [69.4–81.4] Caveat: the book doesn't show everything; orders get pulled before price arrives and some size stays hidden, to be covered later in the series.
- [81.4–97.4] Recap: the DOM shows resting orders, bids left and asks right; market orders execute against them; consumed asks can mean a higher next ask; the book can change any second.
- [97.4–101.7] Next episode: "what is order flow?" Then: follow Doubaz Capital.

### On-screen content, page by page
Every step shows the same illustrative ladder labelled "ILLUSTRATIVE DOM · SIMPLIFIED ORDERS", with a 1-MIN candle on the left. **Asks** (red): 29,392.75 = 31; 29,392.50 = 22; 29,392.25 = 27; 29,392.00 = 18; 29,391.75 = 11; 29,391.50 = 14. **Bids** (green): 29,391.25 = 16; 29,391.00 = 12; 29,390.75 = 25; 29,390.50 = 19; 29,390.25 = 29; 29,390.00 = 34.
**Step 1 (0–4s):** "EVERY CANDLE = EXECUTED TRADES" (a "BUY 5" tag on the candle).
**Steps 2–3 (4–12s):** "SEE THE LIQUIDITY BEHIND THE MOVE".
**Steps 4–6 (12–24s):** "DEPTH OF MARKET", with "← BUYERS WAITING" and "SELLERS WAITING →" labels and "22 contracts waiting at 29,392.50" (the 22 cell is highlighted).
**Steps 7–9 (24–41s):** "BEST BID · BEST ASK": best bid 29,391.25, best ask 29,391.50, and "SPREAD = 1 TICK (0.25 pt = $5 on 1 NQ)". A tag reads "MARKET SELL → HITS THE BID".
**Step 10 (41–45s):** "ASKS AT 29,391.50: 3". The 14 contracts get eaten down by market buys.
**Step 11 (45–50s):** "NEXT ASK MAY BE HIGHER".
**Step 12 (50–55s):** "THE BOOK CHANGES": the ladder shows a refill and a pull ("+6 refilled · 27 pulled"); 29,392.25 is emptied and 29,391.75 shows 15.
**Step 13 (55–60s):** "LIMIT WAITS · MARKET TAKES" ("LIMIT = PASSIVE, waits in the book").
**Step 14 (60–69s):** "PRICE MOVES WHEN AGGRESSION BEATS THE BOOK".
**Steps 15–16 (69–81s):** "THE BOOK DOESN'T SHOW EVERYTHING — orders get pulled · some size stays hidden" (cells marked PULLED and "?"), plus a card: **"COMING IN EP. 15 — What Large Orders Really Mean"**.
**Steps 17–19 (81–97s):** A "DOM CHECKLIST — SAVE THIS" builds up: (1) "Resting orders at each price — that's what the ladder shows"; (2) "Bids left, asks right — buyers waiting · sellers waiting"; (3) "Market orders execute — buys pay the ask, sells hit the bid"; (4) "Next ask may be higher — when the asks at a price are consumed".
**Step 20 (97–105s):** A golden eagle logo and "Next: What Is Order Flow?"

|---|---|---|---|
| NQ tick = 0.25 pt = $5 | contract spec | caption, steps 7–9 | Correct. E-mini Nasdaq-100 (NQ) is $20 per point, so a 0.25 tick is $5 (CME spec). |
| 29,390–29,392.75 prices | numbers | all steps | Illustrative NQ-level prices with made-up sizes. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
It asks only for a follow and a save. There's no link, comment keyword or price in this post. "DOUBAZ Academy" episodes hint at a future education product, but none is named here.

### Verification & insights
- The content is textbook-correct: best bid and ask, the spread, market orders taking liquidity and limit orders providing it, and the fact that price levels clear when resting size is consumed. The caveats about pulled and hidden (iceberg) liquidity are correctly stated.
- The ladder is explicitly illustrative, so it contains no real market data.
- Not verified: who runs Doubaz Capital. The "Futures Trading Desk" label is self-description, and no regulated entity was found.

## DdhrccLtiA_ | @josholdmixon | Satire of "guru" backtests: an NVDA "strategy" turns $100k into $28.2M (Sharpe 16, 0% drawdown) through same-bar look-ahead and zero costs, then collapses to +7.26% (Sharpe 0.16) once "REALITY" is switched on
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DdhrccLtiA_/
- **Account:** @josholdmixon (Josh Oldmixon, Founder & CEO of PentPort) · **Posted:** 2026-09-20 · **Type:** reel (79.3s, 24 scene pages)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram, re-fetched with yt-dlp; identical to the mirror text)
- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE. This is an educational parody. The "hyper-profitable" result is a deliberate look-ahead bug, and the video then shows the realistic result. The CTA only offers the code link, and the creator's company is PentPort.

### Post description
> Watch the full video before making yourself look goofy in the comments
>
> Comment "backtest" and I'll send you the link (must be following)

### Audio
The creator speaks to camera, holding a DJI lav mic. Burned-in word-by-word subtitles. Ironic delivery until the reveal.
- [0.0–10.1] Hook: "I just made this trading strategy that turned $100k into over $28 million", not a 3-month backtest but over 10 years of data back to 2016.
- [10.1–25.6] The strategy: uses NVIDIA's daily price changes to generate a long or short signal, takes a small position (about 10% exposure), and flips direction as the signal changes; the aim is many small profits, not the most profitable trades.
- [25.6–35.9] $100k became over $28 million, about 75% a year compounded over a decade, "but honestly the returns aren't even the craziest part."
- [35.9–47.4] Annualized volatility only 3.53% (max a little over 4), a Sharpe over 16, and execution "cost us nothing. I think my broker is really just the homie."
- [47.4–54.2] No drawdowns at all; he is "so excited to implement this into live trading."
- [54.2–58.9] Reveal: he "forgot to turn this on", says it probably won't change the results much, then: "Oh sh**."
- [58.9–71.6] This is what happens when gurus' strategies include the real world: "instead of 28,000%, we get 7%, with a Sharpe of 0.16. And execution cost us over 6%."
- [71.6–79.2] CTA: for the free link to the code, follow and comment "backtests" below; he posts quant trading videos daily, follow for more. (Whisper wrote "quantum trading"; from context he means quant trading.)

### On-screen content, page by page
The top half is the creator, with subtitle words. The bottom half is a screen recording of a web IDE.
**Page 1 (0.0–4.2s):** Matplotlib chart "Strategy vs. NVDA — Normalized Performance". Y-axis "Normalized Value" 0–25000+, x-axis 2018–2026. The blue Strategy line climbs smoothly to about 28,000. The orange NVDA line ends at about 15,000. Pink overlay text: **"Quant shows how to build hyper-profitable strategy"**.
**Page 2 (4.2–6.6s):** IDE header "Back | **Unicorn Algo** v67 ✎", language selector "Python 3.10.12", tabs "Upload Data · PentPort Docs · Allowed Packa[ges]". Code: `import matplotlib.pyplot as plt` / `from matplotlib.ticker import FuncFormatter, PercentFormatter` / `from pentport import PentPort` / `SYMBOL = "NVDA"` / `INTERVAL = "1day"` / `LOOKBACK = 4000` / `BARS_TO_USE = 2520` / `STARTING_CAPITAL = 100_000` / `GROSS_EXPOSURE = 0.10` / `TRADING_DAYS = 252` / `ROLLING_WINDOW = 252`.
**Page 3 (6.6–10.1s):** The same normalized-performance chart, without the overlay.
**Page 4 (10.1–11.8s):** Full code view. Cost parameters: `QUOTED_SPREAD_BPS = 1.50`, `HALF_SPREAD_BPS = QUOTED_SPREAD_BPS / 2`, `SLIPPAGE_BPS = 1.00`, `MARKET_IMPACT_BPS = 0.50`, `FEES_BPS = 0.10`, `EXTRA_LATENCY_BARS = 1`. Helper functions `response_to_dataframe(response)` (accepts DataFrame/list/dict keys "data", "results", "history", "prices", "candles"; raises "Unexpected PentPort response type"), `parse_timestamp(series)` (ns/us/ms/s detection by median magnitude 1e17/1e14/1e11) and `calculate_turnover(position)`. Right pane: artifact thumbnails (`artifacts/live_fig…`) and an annualized-volatility chart.
**Pages 5–7 (11.8–20.7s):** "Portfolio Value Over Time" chart. Y-axis $0–$25,000,000, rising to about $28M. Legend box: "Ending Value: $28,248,668 · Total Return: 28148.7% · CAGR: 75.6% · Sharpe: 16.02 · Max Drawdown: 0.0% · Annual Volatility: 3.5%". Page 6 overlays the strategy's formulas:
  - r_t = P_t / P_(t−1) − 1
  - s_t = sign(r_t)
  - R_t = 0.10 · s_t · r_t
  - "P_t = NVDA closing price, s_t ∈ {−1, +1}, R_t = strategy return"

| $100k → $28,248,668.45, 28,148.67%, CAGR 75.60%, vol 3.53%, Sharpe 16.02, MDD 0.00% | numbers | audio, p9–p13 | Results of the bugged (look-ahead) run. |
| $107,260.65, +7.26%, CAGR 0.70%, vol 5.01%, Sharpe 0.16, MDD −18.12%, cost 6.09% | numbers | audio 64.9s, p21 | Results of the realistic run. |
| "backtest" | comment keyword | caption, audio | DM link to the code. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "Comment 'backtest' (must be following)" leads to a DM link to this strategy's code. The code imports `pentport` and runs in PentPort's editor, so the link almost certainly points to PentPort (pentport.com), the creator's free paper-trading and competition platform, which is building an order-flow-revenue-sharing brokerage. Following is a growth condition. No price is shown, and no affiliate link was found.
- The real "product" is brand awareness for PentPort plus account sign-ups.

### Verification & insights
- **The trick, decoded:** the strategy goes long when *today's* return is positive and short when it's negative, and earns *today's* return. So R_t = 0.10·|r_t| ≥ 0 on every day. This is pure look-ahead bias ("Same-Bar Signal" in the comparison table). That is why the histogram has no negative bars, drawdown is 0.00%, negative days are 0.00%, and the Sharpe is 16. The 99.84% "positive days" figure (not 100%) reflects zero-return days.
- **Internal consistency:** 282.5× over 10.02 years gives a CAGR of about 75.6%. The stats are internally consistent.
- **"REALITY = True"** applies the one-bar latency (`EXTRA_LATENCY_BARS = 1`, so you trade on yesterday's sign) plus spread, slippage, impact and fees (about 2.35 bps per unit of turnover: 0.75 half-spread + 1.00 + 0.50 + 0.10). With turnover of 259× notional at 10% gross exposure, that comes to about 6.09% cumulative cost. The result is a near-zero-edge daily momentum or reversal bet: +7.26% over 10 years, against NVDA's roughly 150×.
- **Claims vs reality:** the video *intends* to debunk. The message ("guru backtests ignore look-ahead and costs") is correct and is the standard lesson. Note that the spoken "max just a little over 4" refers to the rolling volatility chart (peaks around 4.15%).
- **Dates:** the data ends in late 2026 ("END PRICE $222.27"). That is consistent with a live data feed at the recording date, about Sep 2026.
- **Related:** another entry in this batch (DdFryRpMyTD) covers the same creator and confirms his PentPort bio. On pentport.com the co-founder is Ethan DuBrueler, and the advisors include Tom Dorsey (Dorsey Wright).
- **Not verified:** the content of the DM link, what the "Kwant" assistant is or costs, and whether PentPort's brokerage is live or registered (the site says paper trading is live).

| Parameter stability test / parameter heatmap | method | audio 5.9s, p2–p5 | Standard overfitting check: look for a plateau of good settings, not an isolated spike. |
| "spp heatmap" | term | p8 header | Most likely "strategy parameter permutation" (the funnel diagram's "permute all parameter values" matches the SPP approach popularised in Dave Walton's work on robust strategy evaluation). "spp" is not explained in the video. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Nothing is sold in this post. The CTA is "Follow for more!" (caption). There is no comment keyword, link or product on screen. A web search found nothing about the account.

### Verification & insights
- **Accuracy:** all four steps are mainstream robustness practice (cf. Pardo, *The Evaluation and Optimization of Trading Strategies*; Bailey & López de Prado on backtest overfitting and the Deflated Sharpe Ratio). The overfit-spike vs plateau heatmaps illustrate the idea correctly.
- **Missing pieces the user should know:** no costs or slippage, no multiple-testing correction (testing many parameter sets inflates the best result), no paper-trading or forward-test stage, and no position-size ramp-up. "Once it all checks out you're good to deploy live" overstates the case, because passing these tests reduces but does not remove the risk of overfitting or regime change.
- **The images** appear to be borrowed or AI-generated generic diagrams, not results from the creator's own strategy.
- **Not verified:** the creator's identity or display name, and the exact meaning of "spp".

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- Comment "WAITLIST" → DM link → gammaexposureuniversity.com/waitlist → a 45-second form → you are pushed into a waitlist **Telegram channel** ("you MUST join").
- The waitlist page says the site is operated by **NickelNinjaTradesLLC** (contact nick@ninjagex.com, address 114 Dylan Ave Unit 102, Colchester, VT 05446). It sells a 20-spot mentorship with "early access" and an "early-bird discount", but **no price is shown**. The guarantee reads "Profitable in 90 days, or I'll keep working with you for free until you are." It shows unverified student screenshots ("$35,527.79 day", "+$16,320 past month", "NVDA call closed for +$1,150"). The waitlist closes Oct 9, two days before the Oct 11 public launch. Its own earnings disclaimer says results are "not typical", nothing is guaranteed, and "most traders lose money".
- The ITMatrix plug ("worth every penny") could be affiliate or sponsored, but no referral code was visible and ITMatrix shows no affiliate program.

### Verification & insights
- All four third-party sites are real. Two are free (Forex Factory, FedWatch), ITMatrix has a free tier but its useful tiers cost $29.99–119.99/mo, and Webull is free to open.
- "Consistently profitable in 90 days" is an unverifiable outcome promise. The "guarantee" is only more coaching time, not a refund. Scarcity ("20 people", "for the first time ever", countdown dates) and a Telegram-only channel are classic course-funnel tactics.
- Gamma exposure (GEX) heatmaps are model estimates that assume dealer positioning; they are not observed dealer inventory. Treat them as one input, not a "framework" that guarantees edge.
- The YouTube video title "Risk $38 to Make $310" is a single cherry-picked trade.
- Not verified: mentorship price; Nick Ireland's actual track record; the full caption (429 on refetch).

### Verification & insights
- **Checked:** algovex.app (waitlist and pricing page text; publisher "tacticx Development GmbH"); tacticx.de (tacticx Consulting GmbH, a data-protection and infosec consultancy); hunted.space (the separate algovex.io product). The arithmetic above was recomputed by hand.
- **Realism:** the result is break-even before costs. The panel itself says "costs 0.000%/trade… no costs recorded". With realistic spread, commission and slippage on 1,439 trades, this strategy would almost certainly lose money. It also massively underperformed buy-and-hold (+411.8%).
- To its credit, the reel reports a failure, and the platform's own warning tells users to judge by per-trade expectancy, Sharpe and out-of-sample results rather than compounded total return.
- A "test until one works" series is a multiple-testing trap: after enough tries, one variant will look good by luck. Walk-forward or out-of-sample validation is necessary.
- **Not verified:** the Pro price; the exact instrument and dates of the backtest; the red "38…" tile (probably max drawdown in 2022).

### Post description
> Larry Connors' %B Trading Strategy 33-Year SPY Backtest
>
> If you are interested in Rule-Based Trading, we would love to have you in our Skool community (Link in bio)

### Audio
A narrated voiceover (English, likely TTS-style) that reads the slides. Condensed into timestamped notes with short quotes:
- [0.0–11.7] Intro: Larry Connors' %B strategy is "a simple Bollinger band mean reversion setup" with unusually selective rules, and the latest published SPY backtest shows why it merits a look.
- [11.7–29.6] What %B measures: where price sits relative to its Bollinger Bands; below 0 means under the lower band, above 1 means above the upper band. Connors uses very low readings to spot short-term overextension.
- [29.6–53.9] Rules: close above the **200-day moving average**; %B below **0.20** for **three consecutive days**; buy at the close; exit when %B closes above **0.80**. The article uses a **5-day** Bollinger lookback with **2 standard deviations**.
- [53.9–75.0] Why three readings: one day can be noise, while three in a row demand a more persistent sell-off. The 200-day MA keeps trades inside a broader uptrend, i.e. buying a short-term panic in a longer-term bull market.
- [75.0–90.1] The visual: price stretches below the lower band, entry comes once the condition persists, exit after %B recovers above the upper threshold.
- [90.1–114.7] Backtest (SPY, **1993–2026**): **63 trades**, about **89%** winners, average gain about **1.3%** per trade, profit factor "around 3%" (the slide says ~3.0, so "%" is a misspeak), annual return about **2.5%**, market exposure only **4%**.
- [114.7–131.7] It sat in cash about **96%** of the time: "It waits for a very specific condition and does nothing the rest of the time."
- [131.7–151.3] Limitations: many small winners then an occasional larger loss; "63 trades is still a small sample"; results shift with BB parameters, execution assumptions and the test period; a published strategy can weaken over time.
- [151.3–173.4] Lesson: "%B is [not] magic"; it works best as one component of a broader portfolio, not a complete trading program.
- [173.4–185.8] CTA: invitation to join their rule-based trading community, then like and subscribe.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Skool community (Link in bio)" leads to skool.com/rulebasedtrading, the paid **$99/year** Rule-Based Trading group by Oddmund Groette. The Substack is a second paid product: the %B article itself is "for paid subscribers".

### Verification & insights
- The rules match Connors' published %B setup and Quantified Strategies' own (paywalled) post.
- Realism: the video is unusually candid. About 2.5% a year is far below buy-and-hold SPY (~10%/yr), so the case for the strategy is only its 4% exposure, i.e. a component to combine with others. An 89% win rate with PF ~3 over just 63 trades is a small sample. Mean-reversion tails, such as a 2008- or 2020-style gap, can dominate. Whether costs and slippage are included is not stated.
- There is a misspeak in the audio: "profit factor around 3%". The slide correctly says ~3.0.
- Not verified: the exact backtest figures and the test end date.

When dealers are net long gamma ("positive gamma") they sell rallies and buy dips, which dampens moves; when net short gamma they chase moves, which amplifies them. GEX figures are **estimates** that assume which side dealers are on; they are not observed positions. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- Comment MISS → DM link → the "Apply To Gamma Exposure University" form → considered "if a spot opens up". This is **scarcity marketing**: the cohort is "closed", and you get "first priority". The product is a **$997 one-time** course with lifetime access, according to the site's own code; a cohort of 20 spots was advertised earlier. There is a free Telegram/Discord as a funnel step, plus SMS marketing.
- Connections: his earlier posts DeAO0bpAUM7, DeKLxtVombQ, DeKgYDSgg8t, DeUbqqigAj3 and DenKfMFI9sY push the same course. At its waitlist stage it promised "profitable in 90 days or I'll keep working with you for free".

### Verification & insights
- **Checked:** gammaexposureuniversity.com. The page is rendered by JavaScript, so its main bundle was read as text, not executed: operator NickelNinjaTrades LLC, $997 lifetime price, testimonials and disclaimer. Earlier entries cover his ITMatrix usage and the LLC details.
- **Claims vs reality:**
  - "Charts don't tell you what is moving price" / "dealers will be **obligated** to buy and sell": overstated. Dealers hedge according to models and inventory that nobody outside can see, and GEX levels act as tendencies, not obligations. Academic and practitioner evidence suggests gamma regimes affect intraday volatility, but they are not a standalone edge.
  - The 7-step order (one ticker, one setup, paper trading, grading process over P&L) is decent generic advice, freely available elsewhere (SpotGamma, Tier1Alpha and CBOE educational material explain GEX).
  - The testimonials are cherry-picked individual wins, survivorship bias by construction. The site itself disclaims typical results.
- **Not verified:** his 7-year track record; whether the $997 price still applies; cohort size and availability.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
The CTA ("link in the description" / "Premium for coders: link in bio") leads to a paid ZenomTrader "premium" membership: the "mother EA" with prop-firm rule templates, the challenge simulator with source code, and "build videos, the vault, the bridges, the agents". The format and the explicit "Subscribe" plus channel page suggest @dennisg4368 is either ZenomTrader's own Instagram or a repost of his YouTube Shorts. The account name does not match, so this is not confirmed. The book mentions may be affiliate links ("down there too"). No price was found.

### Verification & insights
- The core message ("your own domain knowledge/judgment survives out of sample better than generic model output") is a reasonable quant maxim. The three books are genuine, respected sources and free to evaluate on their own.
- **Simulator stats:** 14/15 = 93.33% is arithmetically consistent, but:
  - it is a backtest covering only 13 of 102 months (12.75%)
  - there are only 15 samples
  - the average phase-2 pass time is ~127 days
  - it says nothing about live slippage, prop-firm rule changes or out-of-sample periods
- "Everybody else is renting strategies that already stopped working… More for us" is marketing language.
- The upload date and the premium's price and contents could not be verified. No code was executed or obtained.

### Audio
A male narrator speaks in English, with burned-in word-by-word subtitles. These notes are close and timestamped, and every figure is captured.
