- 0:40–1:18: The question was whether, "when QQQ opens strongly during a weaker market regime", late-day strength continues into the close. They wanted a real stop, no overnight risk, and enough trades. There are four parts: a regime filter, an opening gap, a late-session strength filter (where QQQ sits within the day's range), and a same-day exit.
- 1:18–2:05: Costs are a $100k fixed position, $0.005/share/side commission, and $0.01/share/side slippage, about $0.03/share round trip. Results are 205 trades, PF 3.01, 64.9% wins, net about $52,475, average about $256/trade, and max closed-trade DD about $2,615.
- 2:06–2:50: The equity curve shown is **gross** and ends near $62,300. Net is $52,475, and the difference of about $9,826 is modeled costs. The longest losing streak was 7, and 13 of 205 trades were stopped out. The average winner was about $591 and the average loser about $363.
- 3:11–3:40: PF by sub-period: 1999–2007 2.83, 2008–2017 3.28, 2018–2026 2.91, 2022–2026 about 2.75.
- 3:41–4:08: PF by cost level: $0.03 3.01, $0.04 2.80, $0.05 2.61, $0.06 about 2.43. "Nearby settings also remained profitable."
- 4:08–4:45: "the gap itself was not enough". A selective version reached PF about 3.59 with lower DD.
- 4:45–5:42: The rules (see pages 13–14).
- 5:42–6:58: The comparison with the selective version. The narrator says "members only", and that the Skool group has the selective rules, "ready to use code for both strategies across 5 platforms", backtest notes, parameter tests, and rule cards.
- 6:58: He asks viewers to like and subscribe. Any background music is incidental and was not identified.

- Yesterday's QQQ close is below its 50-day moving average.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Link in bio" and the footer lead to **skool.com/rulebasedtrading**, the paid **Rule-Based Trading** Skool community run by Oddmund Groette of Quantified Strategies. It is listed at **$99/year** ("Founding Member", "lock in your rate for life"), with about 365 members at the time of the check. The video gives strategy #1 in full and withholds strategy #2 (the "more selective version") and platform code as members-only bait. The Skool page advertises one backtested strategy per trading day, a strategy library, platform code, and courses. Reviews are few and mixed, and one says strategies are incomplete or unlocked by earning likes. There are no affiliate links. The same funnel appears in this batch's other @quantifiedstrategies posts (DerDA4qlZOE, DerQ3LKinis).

### Verification & insights
- **Internal arithmetic checks out.** 64.9% of 205 is about 133 winners × $591 ≈ $78.6k, and 72 losers × $363 ≈ $26.1k. Net ≈ $52.5k (stated $52,475) and PF ≈ 3.01. 52,475 / 205 ≈ $256 per trade. Gross − costs (62,301 − 9,826) = 52,475. Sub-period trades 65 + 69 + 71 = 205. The stats are coherent, which is not the same as being true out of sample.
- **Return scale.** $52k net over about 27 years on $100k of capital is about $1.9k/yr, roughly 1.9% a year simple. Capital is only in the market for about 1 hour on about 7–8 days a year. The high PF comes from rare, short, selective trades, so it is not a big money-maker on its own. The large PF partly reflects how few trades there are.
- **Costs are realistic for liquid QQQ.** QQQ spreads are about 1¢, so 1¢ per side of slippage is reasonable for market orders at 15:05. Taxes and data costs are excluded.
- **Data-mining risk.** There are four tuned thresholds (50-day MA, 0.60% gap, 15:00 check, 0.77 range) chosen on the same 1999–2026 data they are tested on. The sub-period and nearby-parameter tables help, but they are all in-sample, and the varied parameter on page 11 is never named. There is no true walk-forward or out-of-sample hold-out. This firm publishes a new strategy almost daily, which raises the multiple-testing problem: the best-looking results are what get shown.
- **The equity curve has a single steep jump** around trades 75–80, about 20k→40k, which looks like 2008–2009. A sizeable share of gross profit seems to come from one crisis cluster, although the 2018–2026 PF of 2.91 suggests the effect did not vanish.
- The stop is "1.0% of the $100,000 position", a $1,000 loss. The average loser is $363, so most losses are closed at 16:00, not stopped (only 13 stop exits).
- A minor inconsistency: page 4 says "Exact thresholds and timing stay in the reproducible Skool version", but pages 13–14 then give them. Skool's page says code for 6 platforms, while the video says 5.
- The idea is similar to well-known "last-hour momentum" or intraday-momentum research (Gao, Han, Li, Zhou, "Market Intraday Momentum", JFE 2018), which finds that the first half-hour return predicts the last half-hour.
- **Not verified:** none of the backtest figures could be reproduced (no code or data was supplied, and nothing was run). The selective version is unverifiable by design.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- There is no explicit CTA beyond "save" and "follow".
- No affiliate link was captured, because the caption is empty or missing.
- The Invo segment shows the logged-in app at length. That is typical of referral-style promotion: Invo earns fees on each trade through Hyperliquid's builder codes. No disclosure was seen. Whether this is paid or referral content can't be confirmed without the caption or bio.
- A web search turned up no information on @annatrades45.

### Verification & insights
- **What was checked:** All three sites were confirmed by web search. Invo was confirmed through App Store listings and a CryptoBriefing article. The Messari acquisition was confirmed through a Business Wire release and Cointelegraph coverage.
- **"Gatekept / dangerous / don't search this on Google":** a hook only. All three are mainstream, heavily advertised sites.
- **Finviz claim, "you don't make any bad trades":** FALSE. A screener shows market breadth and movers. It does not prevent losing trades.
- **Invo claim, "copy the trades of the most popular millionaires":** UNSUPPORTED. The traders on screen are pseudonymous accounts with win-rate badges. Nothing shows they are millionaires.
  - Win rate alone ignores position size and leverage: one 40x loss can wipe out many small wins.
  - Copied perpetual-futures trades at 20x–40x leverage can be liquidated quickly.
  - "Automatically open, close and take profit" just means mirroring the leader's position. Profit is not guaranteed.
  - There are trading fees, funding fees and slippage on top.
  - Hyperliquid-based apps may be restricted for some jurisdictions, including the US. Check local rules and the app's terms. This wasn't confirmed here.
- **Messari claim, "see which cryptos are about to blow up before everyone else":** OVERSTATED. Messari offers data and research. It doesn't predict pumps.
- **Better-known equivalents:** TradingView screeners and stockanalysis.com for stocks, and CoinGecko or CoinMarketCap for crypto data.
- **Could not verify:** the caption, the post date, the creator's identity, and any affiliate relationship with Invo.

| Robinhood | broker app | audio 10.9s; tier A | US commission-free broker (stocks, options, crypto, futures). Its revenue partly comes from payment for order flow. |
| thinkorswim | platform | audio 22.9s; tier S | Charles Schwab's (formerly TD Ameritrade's) desktop/mobile platform. Free with a Schwab account. |
| Webull | broker app | audio 33.0s; tier C | US commission-free broker. The creator admits he has never used it. |
| ATAS | order-flow software | audio 39.8s; tier D | Order-flow/footprint charting platform (paid tiers). The claim that it "stole" from Bookmap is opinion with no evidence. Footprint and DOM-heatmap tools are common across the industry. |
| Bookmap | order-flow software | audio 50.3s; tier S | Liquidity-heatmap/order-flow platform (free tier plus paid plans). It can connect to futures data/execution feeds (e.g. Rithmic, CQG) that many futures prop firms use. |
| MetaTrader (MT4/MT5) | platform | audio 60.8s; tier D | MetaQuotes' forex/CFD platform. **Not banned in the US.** FOREX.com offers MT5 to US clients, under US NFA rules (FIFO, no hedging). Apple briefly removed the apps in 2022 and restored them in 2023. |
| Tradovate | futures broker/platform | audio 67.6s; tier A | US futures broker and web platform (owned by NinjaTrader). Widely used by futures prop firms. |
| "The New Age of Day Trading – A-Z Trading Guide" / "The New Age of Technical Analysis" | books | desk props | Creator-branded books. Goodreads lists "The New Age of Technical Analysis" (446 pp., 2022) by the BrandonTrades author. I could not find "The New Age of Day Trading" in a web search. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No call to action in the clip. The books placed in frame are the creator's own products. The BrandonTrades brand (YouTube channel and books) sells trading education, so the reel is audience-building. No affiliate links or codes were seen. Bookmap's praise ("link your prop firms") reads like a common affiliate niche, but no referral was shown.

### Verification & insights
- The rankings are subjective preferences of a futures/order-flow trader. Charting vs execution trade-offs are described fairly for TradingView, Robinhood and Tradovate.
- **False claim:** "MetaTrader is banned in the U.S." MT5 is available to US retail forex clients through FOREX.com, and FTMO relaunched MT5 for US prop traders. MetaTrader is mainly a forex/CFD tool, and CFDs are not allowed for US retail clients, which may be the source of the confusion.
- Ranking Webull C without using it, and ATAS D after "a couple of weeks", is weak evidence.
- Not verified: the creator's real name and the second book. Search results attribute BrandonTrades to an author named on Goodreads, but I did not confirm identity details beyond that listing.

## DX5JVFOxF6e | @julias.algos | Put-call parity explained with a worked $50 stock / $55 strike example and a "$0.54 risk-free" arbitrage, plus the synthetic-forward payoff graph
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DX5JVFOxF6e/
- **Account:** @julias.algos (Julia's Algos) · **Posted:** unknown (not in fetched data) · **Type:** reel (59s, 19 scene pages)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram, fetched after the first review)
- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** PARTLY TRUE – the parity formula and the example math are correct, but "make money 100% risk-free" ignores that such gaps are rarely real for retail (bid-ask spreads, fees, short-borrow cost, dividends, American early exercise) and are arbitraged away by market makers in milliseconds

### Post description
> How do market makers quickly verify an arbitrage opportunity in the market??
>
> One way to do this is with put-call parity! Because markets aren’t always perfectly efficient, sometimes options might be slightly mispriced, creating an opportunity that traders can take advantage of. By repeating the process hundreds or thousands of times, they can expect to make a pretty good profit!
>
> What do you think? Let me know in the comments and follow for more!
>
> #quant #options #finance #optionstrading

### Audio
Creator speaking English to camera:
- [0.0–7.0] "Everyone thinks that option trading is about guessing up or down, but there's actually one simple formula that can help you make money 100% risk-free."
- [7.0–16.0] "There's this rule in options called put-call parity. It's the idea that in a perfectly efficient market, calls and puts with the same strike and expiration must maintain a balance."
- [16.0–19.3] "If that equation doesn't balance, there's an arbitrage opportunity."
- [19.3–30.2] "Let's say XYZ stock is trading at $50. A six-month call is $3. The strike price is $55. Discount that $55 back and it's $54.46."
- [30.2–36.2] "If the put is $7.46, then everything checks out. But if the put is $8, that's a mismatch."
- [36.2–41.8] "You could sell the put, buy the call, sell the stock, and make $0.54 risk-free."
- [41.8–55.5] "Another way that we can model put-call parity is with a synthetic future graph. It's a position that models the exposure of a long futures contract, where you commit to buying stock XYZ at the strike price at expiration regardless of the market price."
- [55.5–58.7] "If you just learned something about options, be sure to like and follow for more."

### On-screen content, page by page
Talking head in a kitchen; burned-in subtitles track the speech.
**Pages 1–2 (0–7s):** Subtitles only ("everyone thinks that option trading…", "there's this rule in options called put call parity").
**Pages 3–4 (7–12s):** Textbook figure "Put-Call Parity": "In market equilibrium, it must be the case that option prices are set such that: C₀ + Xe^(−rT) = P₀ + S₀. Otherwise, riskless portfolios with positive payoffs exist." Payoff diagram (value at expiry vs value of stock at expiry) with lines "Buy a call option with an exercise price of $40", "Buy the stock at $40 financed with some debt: FV = $X", "Sell a put with an exercise price of $40", axis marks $40−P₀, $40, $40+C₀, $40−Xe^(−rT). (Third-party textbook graphic; summarized.)
**Pages 5–6 (12–19s):** Overlay "Call = PV(x)* = Put + Stock" (typo: should read "Call + PV(x) = Put + Stock").
**Page 7 (19–22s):** "let's say XYZ stock is trading at $50".
**Page 8 (22–24s):** "3 + PV(x) = Put + 50".
**Page 9 (24–26s):** "3 + 55 = Put + 50".
**Page 10 (26–30s):** "3 + 54.46 = Put + 50".
**Page 11 (30–33.7s):** "3 + 54.46 = 7.46 + 50".
**Pages 12–14 (33.7–41.8s):** "3 + 54.46 = 8 + 50" – "that's a mismatch", "you could sell the put buy the call…".
**Pages 15–17 (41.8–50s):** Investopedia chart "Put-call parity": profit/loss (−20…20) vs underlying price (0–30); lines "Long call", "Short put", "Forward contract".
**Pages 18–19 (50–59s):** Subtitles only, ending "if you just learned something about options…".

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Put-call parity C + PV(K) = P + S | formula | audio 7–19s, pages 3–6 | Correct for European options on non-dividend stocks (Stoll 1969) |
| XYZ $50, 6-month call $3, strike $55, PV $54.46 | numbers | audio 19–30s, pages 7–10 | Consistent: 55/54.46 ≈ 1.0099 ⇒ ~2% annual rate for 6 months |
| Fair put $7.46; quoted put $8 | numbers | audio 30–36s, pages 11–14 | 3 + 54.46 − 50 = 7.46 ✓ |
| Arbitrage: sell put, buy call, short stock ⇒ $0.54 | rule | audio 36–42s | Correct "reversal": collect 8 − 3 + 50 = $55 now, need only $54.46 invested to cover the $55 purchase at expiry ⇒ $0.54 today (per share, $54 per 100-share contract), before costs |
| "100% risk-free" | claim | audio 3–7s | Only in theory; see below |
| "By repeating the process hundreds or thousands of times, they can expect to make a pretty good profit" | claim | caption | Overstated: real parity violations are tiny, fleeting and mostly eaten by spreads, fees and borrow costs; market makers profit from scale and low costs, not from a reliable stream of free arbitrage |
| #quant #options #finance #optionstrading | hashtags | caption | Topic tags only |
| Synthetic future/forward (long call + short put) | concept | audio 42–55s, pages 15–17 | Correct: long call + short put at the same strike = synthetic long forward at K |
| Investopedia put-call parity chart | source | pages 15–17 | Third-party graphic |
| Textbook "Put-Call Parity" figure | source | pages 3–4 | Third-party textbook diagram (style matches Bodie/Kane/Marcus *Investments*) |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No CTA beyond "like and follow" (audio) and "Let me know in the comments and follow for more!" (caption). The account is an education/creator page (a Motion creator profile lists @julias.algos as a quant-finance/options/algo-trading educator, ~133K Instagram followers, with brand partnerships including OpenAI). No product, course or link in this post.

### Verification & insights
- Math checked by hand: all numbers are internally consistent.
- Overlay error: "Call = PV(x)* = Put + Stock" should be "Call + PV(x) = Put + Stock" (the spoken version is correct).
- Why it's not "100% risk-free" in practice: (1) US single-stock options are American-style, so the short put can be assigned early and parity becomes an inequality; (2) dividends shift parity (subtract PV of dividends from S); (3) shorting the stock needs a borrow and has fees, recalls and hard-to-borrow rates; (4) you trade at bid/ask, not mid, so a $0.54 gap on a $8 put can disappear in the spreads; (5) commissions on 3 legs; (6) apparent violations usually reflect borrow costs or stale quotes. Market makers do use parity checks, which is why real violations are tiny and short-lived.
- Full caption (fetched later) adds the claim that repeating the trade "hundreds or thousands of times" yields "a pretty good profit": this assumes the mispricings are real and exploitable after costs, which for retail they generally are not; it does frame the trade as a market-maker activity, which is accurate.
- Not verified: upload date.

Banner: "JEV GIVES A REASON, NOT A GUARANTEE. Confidence ≠ probability of profit."

- (2) Risk Engine, "Checks if the trade is allowed based on predefined rules": Position size (Based on account risk); Maximum loss limit (Daily / per trade); Portfolio exposure (Total open positions); Correlation check (Avoid overexposure); Stop distance (Logical and realistic); Trading hours (Allowed instruments & time); Kill switch (High volatility / abnormal event).
- (3) APPROVED: ✓ Position size calculated, ✓ Risk limits within range, ✓ Correlation OK, ✓ Execution allowed → "SEND TO EXECUTION". REJECTED, "Trade violates risk rules. Not executed": ✗ Position size too high, ✗ Max loss exceeded, ✗ High correlation, ✗ Outside trading hours.

"Track key metrics": Win rate (% of winning trades), Expectancy (Avg. profit per trade), Drawdown (Maximum drawdown), Sharpe ratio (Risk adjusted returns), Slippage (Execution vs. expected), Execution quality (Fill rate & latency), Market regime (Trending / Ranging), Strategy changes (What improved). Banner: "TRADE → MEASURE → ANALYZE → IMPROVE. Real feedback builds a better trading system."

- 1 Market Data: Stocks / Options, Forex, Crypto, News & Events.
- 2 Feature Engine: Technical indicators, Price & volume features, Statistical features, Market regime detection, Custom features.
- 3 JEV Decision Layer: Analyze market state, Identify opportunities, Explain reasoning, Provide structured signal + confidence.
- 4 Risk Engine: Position size, Max loss limit, Portfolio exposure, Correlation check, Trading hours, Kill switch.
- 5 Execution: Zerodha (Kite), Dhan, Upstox, Angel One, Order management, Position monitoring, Alerts & logs.
Pipeline: BACKTEST (Test the strategy on historical data) → OOS TESTING (Validate on unseen data) → PAPER TRADE (Test in real market conditions) → LIVE (SMALL) (Start with small capital and scale gradually). ### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Comment "JEV" → auto-DM with a PDF ("complete JEV Algo Trading architecture with prompts and starter code"). No price is shown. The creator is a small, early-stage Indian algo-trading account (LinkedIn about 128 followers). The likely aim is list and follower growth, possibly leading to a paid course or group later; nothing paid is visible. There is no affiliate link to TypeSafe, and the post rides the trending "Jev" keyword (the model blew up in late Sep 2026).

### Verification & insights
- Jev was verified by web search (launch coverage, pricing) and through the open re-implementation repo razorback16/openjev (Apache-2.0, last commit 2026-10-12, already cloned in scratchpad/repos/openjev). Its README describes the same "System One" typed-decision API ("answers yes/no, choice, score… parses no text").
- Mismatch: slides 2/4/7 credit Jev with "Explain reasoning" and free-text outputs ("REASON: Trend + momentum aligned…"). Jev outputs probabilities, not prose, so you would have to ask it typed questions (e.g. "long/short/no-trade?" as a choice). The "reasoning" fields would need a separate LLM or your own template.
- The risk-engine and validation advice is correct and is the valuable part. It is generic and model-agnostic: any classifier or LLM could sit in the "decision layer".
- No backtest, P&L or evidence that an LLM/Jev decision layer adds edge over the indicator features fed to it. With an 8-field state built from standard indicators, the "AI" is effectively re-labelling a rules-based trend filter.
- In India, SEBI's 2025 retail algo-trading framework requires broker-registered/approved algos and static IPs for API orders above a threshold, so "starter code" sent via DM may not be compliant out of the box.
- The slides appear AI-generated (stylized icons, uniform layout).
- Not verified: PDF contents, whether "starter code" exists, and the creator's track record.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Join our free community, link in bio." This is a lead magnet for a trading community, probably Discord or Telegram, built around QUANTLAB's automated "bot" that includes the undisclosed third rule. The likely upsell is the bot, signals or a paid tier, but no price or product appeared in the post. The bio link and its domain could not be retrieved (Instagram profile not fetched), and a web search for the handle found nothing.

### Verification & insights
- **Consistency check (reasoning only, not executed):** at a 2R target with a 50.8% win rate, a full-target-or-full-stop model gives about +0.52R per trade. 1,908 trades × 0.52R × 0.75% ≈ +745% simple, which is far above the stated +312.9% (compounded would be higher still). So many trades must exit at the 24-hour time limit below target, and the realised average win is well under 2R. The headline "the target is twice the risk" explains less than implied; the time exit drives results. Average R per trade, spread and financing are not shown.
- **Costs:** UK100 CFDs carry a spread (often about 1 point) plus overnight financing on positions held past the cut-off. Neither is mentioned, and the 24h holds incur overnight funding.
- **Overfitting:** the creator openly says thresholds were chosen in-sample, which is good disclosure. "Results hold when thresholds move" is shown only as one equity chart, with no out-of-sample period.
- **"Guarantee"** in the caption is marketing; the voiceover itself backs off ("I don't mean richer"). A backtest is not a guarantee.
- **Monday effect / short-term RSI mean reversion in indices:** a known pattern family (e.g. Connors-style RSI pullbacks). It is plausible as a modest edge before costs, but crowded and regime-dependent.
- **Not verified:** the bio link, the community, the bot, and the backtest itself.

  - "Footprint Chart (What happened at 2351.50?)". Rows of 4 cells each: 2352.00: 32 45 55 28 · 2351.75: 48 90 82 35 · **2351.50: 120 210 250 180** · 2351.25: 95 60 70 55 · 2351.00: 40 35 28 20. - **④ Why VAP is Important in Order Flow Trading?**
  1. "Find important prices": high-volume prices become key levels where market participants were active; price often reacts when it returns to these levels.
  2. "Identify HVN and LVN": HVN (High Volume Node) → acceptance / balance → price spends time here. LVN (Low Volume Node) → rejection / fast move → price traded here quickly.
  3. "Understand market behavior": large volume shows strong participation; you can check footprint and DOM to see who was aggressive and who was absorbed.
  4. "Better trade decisions": helps you find potential support/resistance; helps you understand whether a breakout has real participation or not.
  5. "Works with other tools": VAP = location of trading activity; Footprint = behavior at that location; DOM = execution and current liquidity.
- **⑤ Important Points to Remember:** ☑ "High volume does NOT always mean buy." ☑ "Low volume does NOT always mean sell." ☑ "VAP shows where business happened, you still need footprint and DOM to understand what it means." ☑ "Look for confluence with market structure, trend, and context."
- **Key Takeaway:** "Volume at Price tells you the exact price levels where the most trading happened. It helps you identify important levels, understand market behavior, and make better decisions when combined with footprint and DOM."

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No call to action, link or product in the post. The same account as Dd58nMdpItj, posting a series of free order-flow infographics. No paid offer was found by web search.

### Verification & insights
- The method (wait at a volume-profile level, look for absorption or icebergs, enter on a shift in aggression, stop beyond the level) is a mainstream order-flow scalping description. Bookmap, Jigsaw and Axia teach very similar material for free or in paid courses.
- Errors and caveats:
  - Panel 1 shows resting bids (432) and asks (492) at the **same** price (2328.0). In a real DOM the best bid is always below the best ask; equal prices would trade at once. This is a sign of AI-generated content.
  - Spoofing and pulled liquidity (which the slide itself names as "pulling") make DOM stacking unreliable. The slide's rule 3 ("Don't trade just because a big order appears") partly admits this.
  - No win rate, sample or costs are given. Scalping with tight stops is very sensitive to commissions and slippage. Most retail futures scalpers lose money, per broker and exchange disclosures in general.
  - With R:R of 1:1.5–1:2, you need a win rate above about 33–40% just to break even before costs.
- Not verified: the account's identity, and whether any course exists behind the account.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No explicit call to action beyond "bookmark" and "comment". The reel names one platform and its URL, frames it as a free discovery, and uses an "I just found this" format. That pattern is common in paid or affiliate creator placements. No #ad tag or affiliate code is visible, and no InsiderFinance affiliate programme was found on its pricing page. The full product is paid ($55–75/month).

### Verification & insights
- **GEX strike walls:** a real, widely used concept. Large positive dealer gamma near a strike tends to dampen moves, so price "pins" or stalls there, especially near expiry. The effect is probabilistic and depends on assumptions: GEX models assume dealers are long calls and short puts, which is often wrong. 0DTE open interest also changes during the day.
- **The "volatility skew" claim is wrong as stated.** An IV smile shows how implied volatility varies across strikes. It does not "identify the expected intraday range so you know exactly where the low and high sit". The creator reads levels where the curve crosses zero; that curve is more likely a rich/cheap or bid/under-fair overlay than a range forecast. The legitimate range estimate on that page is the 0DTE expected move (1.0% ≈ ±$7 on QQQ at $717), which is far wider than the 717.5–720.5 he cites.
- **Evidence:** one session, marked after the fact ("this was the high, this area we stalled"). Cherry-picked hindsight with no out-of-sample test.
- The creator admits: "Most of the concepts make sense but I just don't really understand how to use it."
- Free alternatives for GEX levels exist (for example FlashAlpha's free tool, and community TradingView scripts), as do SpotGamma's free educational pages.
- Not verified: whether the post is sponsored, and the exact trading date (around early September 2026).

Example: target **20%** with Bitcoin at **60%** → hold **one-third** of capital; if volatility spikes to **90%**, size drops to about **22%** automatically.
- [61.5–73.8] Why it survives crashes: smaller when markets are violent, bigger when calm and trending. **Rebalance weekly, exit when the signal flips**; that's the whole system.
- [74.8–81.4] Downside: it loses in choppy markets; trend strategies win big on a few trends and bleed in ranges.
- [82.8–92.4] He says he is backtesting it on Bitcoin and will post the real numbers, wins and drawdowns. CTA: follow for results and **comment "trend"** for the full rules.

"BTC VOLATILITY" gauge animating 60% → 84% → **90%**. "POSITION SIZE OF CAPITAL" ring 0% → 32% → **33%**, then **22%**. Lines: "**20% ÷ 60% = 33% POSITION**" / "**20% ÷ 90% = 22% POSITION**".
**Card 5 (61.5–73.8s): "WHY IT WORKS · SURVIVES CRASHES".** "BTC PRICE" with a red "CHAOS" zone (price drop) then a green "CALM" zone (recovery). "POSITION SIZE" bars shrink in chaos and grow back. "**CHAOS → SMALLER** / **CALM TREND → BIGGER**". "**REBALANCE WEEKLY**" with a progress bar and an "EXIT" toggle.
**Card 6 (74.8–92.7s): "⚠ THE HONEST PART".** "TRADE P&L – EQUITY" chart: many small red losses, three large green wins, a stair-step equity line. "THE TRADE-OFF: ⚠ **CHOP = SMALL LOSSES** / ↗ **TRENDS = BIG WINS**". "BACKTEST · BTC · • RUNNING" with tags "REAL NUMBERS · WINS · DRAWDOWNS". "✓ FOLLOWING FOR THE RESULTS" / "💬 **COMMENT "TREND"**". Footer "@tradrquant · NOT FINANCIAL ADVICE".

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Time-series momentum (TSMOM) | strategy | audio 9.0s; card 2 | Real and well documented: Moskowitz, Ooi & Pedersen, "Time Series Momentum" (JFE 2012), 58 futures; Hurst, Ooi & Pedersen, "A Century of Evidence on Trend-Following Investing" (AQR). The classic version uses a **12-month** lookback and a 40% per-asset vol target. |
| BTC, 30-day lookback | parameter | audio 20.7s; card 3 | The creator's choice. Short lookbacks trade more and get chopped more. Crypto momentum has academic support (e.g. Liu & Tsyvinski, "Risks and Returns of Cryptocurrency", RFS 2021). |
| Long if 30-day return > 0, flat (or short on perps) if < 0 | rule | audio; card 3 | Fully specified signal |
| Size = target vol ÷ current vol; 20% target | formula | audio 44.6s; card 4 | Standard volatility targeting (cf. Moreira & Muir, "Volatility-Managed Portfolios", JF 2017). Arithmetic is correct: 20/60 = 33.3%, 20/90 = 22.2%. |
| BTC volatility 60% / 84% / 90% | numbers | card 4 | Illustrative annualised vols. BTC's realised vol has historically been roughly 40–80% annualised. |
| Rebalance weekly; exit when the signal flips | rules | audio 69.5s; card 5 | Execution schedule |
| "Survives crashes" | claim | card 5 | Partly true: vol targeting de-risks *after* vol rises. Gap moves (e.g. an overnight or weekend crash) still hit before the resize. |
| "Chop = small losses, trends = big wins" | claim | card 6 | An accurate description of trend following's payoff profile (low win rate, positive skew) |
| Backtest BTC "running" | claim | card 6 | **No numbers shown.** The results are promised in future posts. |
| Comment "TREND" | CTA | audio 89.7s; card 6 | DM with the "full rules" |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment TREND" triggers an auto-DM with the "full rules". "Follow for the results" is the follower-growth hook (a backtest series). No product, price, link or broker/exchange referral is visible in the clip. A web search found no site or profile info for "tradrquant". Typical next steps for such accounts are a newsletter, a paid strategy or indicator, or an exchange affiliate link, but none is shown here.

### Verification & insights
- **What's right:** TSMOM and volatility targeting are genuine institutional techniques with decades of evidence across asset classes. The sizing arithmetic is correct. The "honest part" (losses in ranging markets) is accurate.
- **What's missing or misleading:**
  - No backtest results yet. "This is a real hedge fund strategy" refers to the family of strategies, not to this exact 30-day BTC rule, which has no published track record here.
  - **Leverage is unbounded.** If BTC vol falls below 20%, the formula asks for more than 100% of capital. A cap (e.g. 1×) is needed.
  - Unspecified: how "current vol" is measured (window, e.g. 20 or 30 days; EWMA; annualisation), the day and time of the weekly rebalance, whether to exit mid-week on a flip, and fees, slippage and perp **funding** costs (shorting perps costs or earns funding).
  - Single-asset trend following (BTC only) has much higher drawdowns than diversified CTA portfolios, whose edge comes partly from diversifying across about 50+ markets.
  - Short-lookback signals on one asset are prone to whipsaw and to data-mined parameter choice (why 30 days and 20%?).
- Not verified: the full caption and any future backtest numbers. No code or data was shown, so nothing was executed.

### Post description
> Comment “WAITLIST” to get on the waitlist for GAMMA EXPOSURE UNIVERSITY before I launch it to the public on October 11th

### Audio
Creator speaking to camera (English). Whisper mis-hears are corrected in brackets. Condensed to timestamped notes:
- [0.0–2.7] Hook: "Let's rank options concepts from goat to trash."
- [2.7–12.1] Max pain ("Max Payne" in the transcript): **trash**. It is just the contract with the most open interest, not a force; dealers ("Steelers") aren't obliged to pin price there. "Treating it as a target is numerology."
- [12.1–20.6] Unusual options flow: **mid**. "A print is not positioning": you don't know if it was opened, closed or hedged; without more context it is noise.
- [20.6–30.8] Implied volatility: **A plus**. It shows how expensive premium is relative to itself; no direction, but it tells you whether you are paid to sell convexity or overpaying to buy it.
- [30.8–40.7] Put/call ratio ("Quick to call" in the transcript): **not needed**. It is just volume divided by volume; a high number can be fear, a hedge or a short put, and the ratio can't tell you which.
- [40.7–60.7] Gamma exposure: **goat tier**. It maps dealer convexity across the surface. In positive gamma, hedging is counter-cyclical (dealers buy dips, sell rips; volatility falls and price gets pinned). In negative gamma it is pro-cyclical (dealers sell into weakness, buy into strength). "Every other concept on this list is a clue. This one is the constraint underneath the clue."
- [63.1–71.6] Call to action: "For the first time ever, I'm opening Gamma Exposure University for 20 people who wanna become consistently profitable by working directly with me. Comment waitlist to save your spot."

### On-screen content, page by page
The layout is the creator on camera at the top with word-by-word subtitles, and a tier-list board below. Tier rows from top to bottom: "GOAT", "A+++", "mid", "not needed", "trash".
**Page 1 (0–~12s):** Empty tier list. A max-pain chart (an open-interest bar histogram by strike, U-shaped, with a marked strike) is placed into "trash".
**Pages 2–3 (~12–63s):** The board fills as he talks. "mid" gets a dark "Realtime Option Flow" table (unusual-flow scanner rows with green and red call/put tags). "A+++" gets a white implied-volatility line chart. "not needed" gets a dark Bloomberg-style chart titled "Disturbing Trades" about put volume and the put/call ratio. "GOAT" gets a purple gamma-exposure heat-map grid (strikes × expiries). Subtitle snippets include "Gamma Exposure", "straight to GOAT", "counter-cyclical", "Volatility", "The hedging is pro", "sell into weakness", "is a clue".

### Verification & insights
- The educational content is mostly sound and conventional. His dismissal of max pain and the put/call ratio, and his caution about raw flow, are defensible.
- "Consistently profitable" is a results promise with no evidence. No P&L, track record or audited performance is shown.
- GEX numbers depend on the data vendor and on assumptions (for example, that dealers are short puts and long calls). Treat GEX as a contextual regime indicator, not a "constraint".
- The same GEX framework is free to learn from SpotGamma's free resources, SqueezeMetrics' white paper "Gamma Exposure (GEX)" and many TradingView open-source GEX scripts.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Commenting "MODEL" triggers an auto-DM promising to show "what this model predicts every day". In practice that is a daily efficient/neutral/inefficient forecast for NQ, most likely delivered through a link to a newsletter, Discord, Telegram or website signup that collects your email or handle. It is likely the top of a funnel toward a paid signal service, community or strategy. No price or product is shown and a web search found none. The underlying trading strategy is never disclosed.

### Verification & insights
- The arithmetic was cross-checked and is internally consistent. The bucket-weighted expectancy (254×154 + 257×281 + 318×454) / 829 = $308.4 matches the $308 "without" figure. "Low efficiency days made about a third of the money per trade" checks out: $154 / $454 = 0.34. Full-period return/drawdown 255,710 / 28,259 = 9.05 matches.
- **Omission:** over the full 2020–2026 sample the gate removes 254 trades that were still profitable ($154 each). Total profit with the gate is about 575 × $377 ≈ $216.8k (also 9.12 × $23,734 ≈ $216.5k), against $255.7k without, a **drop of roughly $39k (−15%)**. The full-period table omits the total-profit row. Only the 2025–26 out-of-sample table shows total profit, and there it rose (+$8,570). "Improved almost every performance metric" is true for the risk-adjusted ratios but not for total P&L over the full period.
- The full-period Sharpe barely changed (1.42 → 1.43) and return/drawdown hardly moved (9.05 → 9.12). The large improvements appear only in the shorter out-of-sample window (about 147 vs 208 trades), where the noise is larger.
- The backtest is hypothetical and its costs, slippage, contract size and position sizing are not stated. The base strategy is secret, so nothing is reproducible. The bucket split appears to use the full 829 trades, which overlap the training period. Only the final table is labelled as unseen data.
- The idea itself (regime filtering by predicted trendiness) is reasonable and the model is honestly described ("logistic regression — not a neural network"). The spoken opener "predicts how the market will move" overstates it: the model predicts trendiness, not direction, as he says next.
- Not verified: the account's identity, any product or price, and live trading results.

## DePoq9qovVj | @davidtrades_model | Phone-filmed scroll through three real intraday-momentum papers (Gao et al. 2018; Baltussen et al. 2021; Beckmeyer & Moerke 2021): "the first 30 minutes predict the last 30", with a "comment 30" DM funnel
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DePoq9qovVj/
- **Account:** @davidtrades_model (David) · **Posted:** 2026-10-08 · **Type:** reel (58.0s, 6 pages)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram, fetched with yt-dlp)
- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** PARTLY TRUE. The three papers are real, correctly attributed and freely available on SSRN, and the effect they document is genuine academic evidence. But the "making money trading in 30 minutes a day" framing overstates it. These are small, cost-sensitive return-predictability effects in in-sample periods ending 2013 to 2020, not a proven retail strategy. The "comment 30" funnel only gates free papers.

### Post description
> Making money trading in 30 minutes a day? It sounds like the classic claim from your favorite guru. The difference is that this time there's academic research behind it 📄📊

- From the papers he consults every week, three point at the same window: **the last 30 minutes of the session**.
- **Paper 1** (Journal of Financial Economics): the S&P 500 ETF, **1993–2013**; the first half hour of the day predicts the last half hour.
- **Paper 2:** the same idea on **over 60 futures contracts** (stocks, bonds, commodities, currencies), **1974–2020**; the pattern appears everywhere.
- **Paper 3:** explains who pays for it: option market makers hedging their books into the close.
- **Contrast:** "everyone is trading the opening range breakout", built on the first 30 minutes; the authors say "the first 30 minutes are the signal, the last 30 are the trade." That assumption underlies a strategy he is currently testing.
- **Call to action:** comment "30" for the three papers by DM, "with the rule in plain language and the numbers you need to check before you believe it 🔗".
- **Disclaimer:** educational content only, not financial advice; past performance does not guarantee future results; trading involves substantial risk of loss.
- **Hashtags:** #intradaymomentum #tradingstrategies #quanttrading #backtesting #tradingeducation

*Caption condensed – full text is on the original post.*

### Audio
English voice-over by the creator (no face shown). Word-by-word subtitles are burned in. Condensed to timestamped notes:
- [0.0–10.7] Hook: "Making money trading in 30 minutes a day?" It sounds like a guru claim, but "there's scientific research behind it."
- [10.7–19.6] From the papers he constantly consults he found three interesting ones, shown one after another ("the first is this one…").
- [19.6–28.6] All rest on "an incredible assumption" that let him develop a strategy he is currently testing.
- [28.6–38.3] All three highlight one time window: the last 30 minutes of the market.
- [38.3–54.1] Everyone trades the opening range breakout, based on the first 30 minutes; these authors say the opposite, "that the first 30 minutes of the session predict the last 30 minutes", with "really interesting returns" documented in the papers.
- [54.1–58.0] Call to action: "Comment 30 below and I'll send you the three papers in DM."

### On-screen content, page by page
All of it is filmed with a phone pointed at a laptop screen. Above the laptop, a second monitor shows a candlestick/line chart labelled "E-mini" (E-mini futures), with green horizontal box lines that look like an opening-range box. The browser UI is in Italian ("Profili", "Scheda", "Finestra", "Aiuto"). Open tabs include "Hedging Demand…", "Market Intraday M…", "Algorithmic Tradi…", "AQR Momentum…", "Search Results" and "Time Series…".
**Page 1 (0.0–13.2s):** A PDF ("…Full-Paper_id-237.pdf", page 1/46, zoom 164%). Title "End-of-Day Momentum in the Cross-Section and Option Hedging", Heiner Beckmeyer, Mathis Moerke, "This version: January 07, 2021". The abstract is visible (third-party text, summarised): option market makers delta-hedging single-stock options create temporary price pressure in the last half hour, with momentum or reversal of "18 basis points for each one percentage point move" up to 30 minutes before the close. The direction depends on market makers' aggregate gamma exposure, and the effect is stronger for large caps. JEL G12, G13, G14, G23. Keywords: Intraday Momentum, Cross-Sectional Momentum, Gamma Exposure, Option Market Maker. Subtitle: "making money in".
**Page 2 (13.2–17.2s):** The same PDF, the hand scrolls. Subtitle: "consult".
**Page 3 (17.2–37.5s):** The SSRN page abstract_id=3760365, by Martin Martens (Robeco Quantitative Investments), "Date Written: January 2, 2021". Summary of the abstract: hedging short gamma requires trading in the direction of price moves. Using intraday returns on over 60 futures on equities, bonds, commodities and currencies between 1974 and 2020, the paper documents strong "market intraday momentum" everywhere: the last-30-minute return is predicted by the return over the rest of the day, and the effect is linked to hedging demand from options and leveraged-ETF market makers. Keywords: Return momentum, Futures trading, Hedging demand, Return Predictability, Indexing. JEL G12, G15, G40, Q02. Suggested citation: Baltussen, Guido; Da, Zhi; Lammers, Sten; Martens, Martin, "Hedging Demand and Market Intraday Momentum" (January 2, 2021), Journal of Financial Economics (JFE), Volume 142, Issue 1, October 2021, Pages 377–403, SSRN 3760365, doi 10.2139/ssrn.3760365. The "Download This Paper" and "Open PDF in Browser" buttons are visible. Subtitles: "second is this", "and the third", "they're all based…", "that allowed me", "I'm currently testing", "highlight a very", "minutes of the".
**Page 4 (37.5–42.5s):** The SSRN page for paper 2440866, Washington University in St. Louis, John M. Olin Business School. "There are 2 versions of this paper", "Date Written: June 19, 2017". Summary of the abstract: with high-frequency S&P 500 ETF data from 1993 to 2013, "the first half-hour return on the market since the previous day's market close predicts the last half-hour return" (highlighted). The effect is stronger on volatile, high-volume, recession and macro-news days. It is also present in ten other actively traded domestic and international ETFs, and is consistent with Bogousslavsky's (2016) infrequent-rebalancing model and with late-informed trading near the close. Keywords: Predictability, Intraday, Momentum, Economic Value. JEL G11, G14. Citation: Gao, Lei; Han, Yufeng; Li, Sophia Zhengzi; Zhou, Guofu, "Market Intraday Momentum" (June 19, 2017), SSRN https://ssrn.com/abstract=2440866. Subtitle: "the market".
**Page 5 (42.5–55.0s):** A close-up of the same abstract. Subtitle: "on the first".
**Page 6 (55.0–58.0s):** A blurred background with an Instagram-style "Write a comment…" box. Text: "comment 30 below".

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gao, Han, Li, Zhou, "Market Intraday Momentum", SSRN 2440866 | paper | pages 4–5; caption ("the first one, published in the JFE") | Real. Published in the Journal of Financial Economics 129(2), 2018, pp. 394–414. S&P 500 ETF (SPY) 1993–2013. The caption's description is accurate. |
| Baltussen, Da, Lammers, Martens, "Hedging Demand and Market Intraday Momentum", JFE 142(1), Oct 2021, pp. 377–403, SSRN 3760365 | paper | page 3; caption ("over 60 futures… 1974 to 2020") | Real and correctly cited. Note that this paper already attributes the effect to hedging by option and leveraged-ETF market makers, not only the "third" paper. |
| Beckmeyer & Moerke, "End-of-Day Momentum in the Cross-Section and Option Hedging" (version Jan 7, 2021) | paper | pages 1–2; caption ("the third one… option market makers hedging") | Real SSRN working paper (title, authors and abstract read from the on-screen PDF; publication status not checked). Stock-level effect of 18 bps per 1 pp move, with direction set by market-maker gamma. |
| Opening range breakout (ORB) | strategy concept | audio 38s, caption | A common day-trading setup built on the first 30 minutes' high and low. The papers do not test or contradict ORB as such. They study a different (close-of-day) predictability. |
| "E-mini" chart; browser tabs "AQR Momentum", "Algorithmic Trading", "Time Series…" | screen detail | all pages | Background props. Nothing is identifiable beyond the labels. |
| Bogousslavsky (2016) | paper (cited inside abstract) | pages 4–5 | Real: "Infrequent Rebalancing, Return Autocorrelation, and Seasonality", Journal of Finance 2016. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- Comment "30" → an auto-DM with the three papers plus "the rule in plain language and the numbers you need to check". All three papers are **free on SSRN** (the IDs above), so you do not need the DM.
- The creator says he is "currently testing" a strategy on this premise, but no results are shown. No product or price is mentioned in this post. An earlier post by the same account (DdMkrwVpwNa) funnelled "comment EDGE" to a free data/backtesting site that appears to be London Strategic Edge. The comment-keyword DM flow is mainly a reach and lead-capture mechanism (comments boost the post, and the DM opens a channel for later offers).

### Verification & insights
- All three citations were checked against the on-screen SSRN pages and the known journal records. The titles, authors, sample periods (1993–2013 SPY; 1974–2020 for 60+ futures) and JFE volume/pages are correct.
- **"Making money in 30 minutes a day":** overstated. These papers show *statistical predictability* of the last-half-hour return. Gao et al. report a timing strategy with modest gains, and the predictability is stronger on volatile and news days. Real trading would pay spreads and commissions on every daily round trip, and the edge per trade is small (basis points).
- **Out of sample:** the US SPY sample ends in 2013. International follow-ups find the effect in many markets (12 of 16 in one study) but weak or absent in some Asia-Pacific markets. No clean post-2013 US decay study was found in this check. Effects that are widely published and tied to hedging flows can vary with market-maker gamma positioning, which is the very mechanism the third paper describes. When dealers are long gamma, the effect can reverse.
- **"Everyone trades ORB, these authors say the opposite":** this is a rhetorical contrast. The papers make no claim about ORB.
- No numbers, backtest or strategy rules are shown in the video. The "strategy I'm currently testing" cannot be verified.
- Sources: [Gao et al. on mql5 mirror](https://c.mql5.com/forextsd/forum/173/intraday_momentum_-_the_first_half-hour_return_predicts_the_last_half-hour_return.pdf), [international intraday momentum follow-up (Reading repository)](https://reading-9.eprints-hosting.org/95566/1/Accepted-Version.pdf), [QuantConnect intraday ETF momentum replication](https://www.quantconnect.com/research/15348/intraday-etf-momentum/p1).

## DerjUDcBPSa | @tradingelder | Phone-filmed walkthrough of a DeepCharts order-flow layout (Deep Print footprints, Deep Stats, Big Trades settings) plus an absorption example
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DerjUDcBPSa/
- **Account:** @tradingelder (display name not retrieved) · **Posted:** 2026-10-19 (decoded from the post ID; ~15:12 UTC) · **Type:** reel (95.7s, 17 scene pages)
- **Caption source:** possibly truncated (mirror). The caption is 102 characters and does not end in "…", so it looks complete. A full re-fetch got HTTP 429.
- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** PARTLY TRUE. The settings and concepts (footprint, delta, big-trade bubbles, absorption) are real DeepCharts features. "See the actual market participants in real time" oversells it, and the CTA leads to a live "class" that sells the tool.

### Post description
> I’m going live this week going over how to get and use this tool, comment “class” for your free invite

### Audio
The creator's own voiceover (English), over the phone-filmed screen. Condensed into timestamped notes with short quotes:
- [0.0–4.3] Hook: these are his order-flow settings that let him "see the actual market participants in real time."
- [4.3–15.4] Main view: a **volume footprint chart overlaid with big orders**, showing big aggressive orders entering the market and being filled by passive participants.
- [15.4–26.6] Footprint 1: **delta footprint** set to **profile mode** (bigger bar = more volume), input data set to **volume**.
- [26.6–37.8] He overlays **two footprint charts**: one shows total volume, the other the numbers inside the bars; the second is set to type **ask/bid split**, also on volume.
- [37.8–53.5] Bottom panel: **deep stats**, showing each candle's total volume, total **delta** (aggressive buyers minus sellers) and the **delta % change** between candles.
- [53.5–63.8] Indicator **big trades**, showing large aggressive participants in real time, set to **aggregate trades**.
- [63.8–78.6] Key settings: **standard deviation 2.5, minimum size 1.2, maximum size 6.0**. This is how he spots "aggressive sellers getting rewarded and aggressive buyers getting trapped or absorbed."
- [78.6–88.4] Chart read: **−3.3k** delta, price moves down; **+2.5k** delta; a big order with no move up, a neutral close, then price falls.
- [88.4–97.6] Example: big aggressive sellers and strong negative delta, but price doesn't fall: "Absorption, price moves up."

### On-screen content, page by page
The reel is a phone video of a monitor. A burned-in title, **"The best orderflow setttings"** (sic, three t's), stays on screen the whole time.
**Page 1–2 (0.0–15.1s):** A live footprint chart in purple and green. Cyan (ask) and magenta (bid) circles mark big trades. The creator's hand points at the screen.
**Page 3–5 (15.1–26.3s):** The **"Deep Print"** settings dialog (toggle on). Chart area: Horizontal (selected) / Vertical, "1". Left menu: GENERAL, PLOT SETTINGS, POC, VALUE AREA, ALERT SETTINGS, SINGLE PRINT, RATIO, VOL CLUSTER, PULLBACK, SIZING (MULTI COLUMN). GENERAL › TYPOLOGY: **Type = Delta, Mode = Profile**. PROFILE PROPORTION: Proportion Type = Bar relative; Proportion Std. Dev (Auto) ≈ 1.0 (blurry); Proportion Value (Manual) = 1. DATA SETTINGS: **Input data = Volume**; Filter min = 0; Filter max = 0; "Use mbo for trades counter" = off. TICK GROUPING collapsed. Bottom: "Indicator Template", "Protected" toggle off, Apply / OK.
**Page 6–7 (26.3–33.6s):** The platform's indicator panel (top bar shows a "774" count). Chart Area 1 holds **Deep Print (2), Deep Print (3), Big Trades (4)**; Chart Area 2 holds **Deep Stats (5)**.
**Page 8 (33.6–39.8s):** The second Deep Print dialog: **Type = Ask bid split**, Mode = Box (blurry), Proportion Type = Bar relative, Input data = Volume, Filter min/max = 0.

**Page 11–12 (52.5–63.5s):** The Big Trades dialog, ALERT SETTINGS: Automatic – Intensity = Low; Manual – Filter Min = a 3-digit value, unreadable (looks like 1x0); Manual – Filter Max = 0.

COLOR: Minimum opacity and Maximum opacity (values blurry); **Ask color = cyan; Bid color = magenta**.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment 'class'" gets a DM invite to a live session on "how to get and use this tool". The tool is DeepCharts, a paid subscription platform, and the indicators are DeepCharts-specific. DeepCharts runs an affiliate program, so the class is most likely a sign-up funnel (affiliate link and/or a paid mentorship). No price was found, and web searches turned up no public site or course for @tradingelder.

### Verification & insights
- DeepCharts was checked via deepcharts.com, Optimus Futures and the v15.6 changelog. Deep Prints and Big Trades are real DeepCharts features. Newer versions add "Deep Trades", an MBO-based tool.
- The settings shown (delta footprint in profile mode plus an ask/bid split footprint, Big Trades sized by standard deviation) are ordinary footprint configurations. Nothing proprietary is revealed beyond the bubble sizes.
- The claim of seeing "actual market participants in real time" is overstated. Footprints show aggregated executed volume, not identities. Big Trades on "aggregate trades" reconstructs prints and can mislabel iceberg or split orders.
- The absorption example (heavy selling, price doesn't fall, then it rises) is a single hindsight-picked example. No win rate or backtest is given.
- Not verified: the creator's identity, the class price, the exact symbol, and the alert filter-min value.
